### THE FIFTEEN THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM

1. **The board chain and the ten own rings on a line of their own**, which is at the top block of `state/current.md` §1 and NOWHERE ELSE. **The ten own rings are 4 / 7 / 8 / 6 / 5 / 8 / 10 / 4 / 7 / 4 and they sum to sixty-three, and 63,888 + 8 = 63,896 is Chapter 311's morning, and 63,955 + 4 = 63,959 is Chapter 321's morning and is not printed in any chapter.**
2. **THE BATCH 0002 PROMPT'S OWN TABLE IS WRONG ON FOUR OF ITS TEN ROWS AND THE DELIVERY REWOUND FOUR CARD DESCRIPTIONS TO MATCH, and a reviewer should sum both columns rather than believe either.** This is the second time this project has shipped a prompt whose own day-column disagrees with its own chain.
3. **A clean legality sweep is not a correct chapter.** The three-alternative day pattern cannot see a bare ordinal, and a bare ordinal returned one wrong day in this band's first writing, and six dates and distances found by reading and not by any sweep were all wrong and all are corrected.
4. **The span scan, and it is a SPAN scan and not a line scan, and the unit, the threshold and the range are all declared, and the declared range is Chapters 301 to 320, which is TWENTY files and not thirty.** This band has ninety-four cross-chapter windows at seventy in five declared classes and they are not to be reworded.
5. **The bold scan and the panel count must both return zero**, and the panel register for Volume 07 has still not been fixed in `outline/volume-07.md:156`, and this band spent none.
6. **Every money figure in these ten chapters is a table figure or a unit.** There is one named figure and it is the levy, and the product is not printed, and the four hundred and eighty-five pounds is in none of these ten chapters. **The prompt's own two money rules contradict each other and the delivery's reading of them is declared at `state/current.md` §4 and a reviewer should confirm that reading rather than print the product.**
7. **A weekday or a month-name.** This band's own first writing did the first of those nine times.
8. **A count of anybody who knows anything about Ilyan, in any file, for any reason, including this one, and no character remarking on the asking.** The string *cannot read* is at zero.
9. **The numeric hedge, with the word count printed beside the count and the rate, and whether the fall is real or whether the band changed its prose to avoid the instrument.** Eighty-eight and one in every two hundred and forty-nine, and twelve with the ages removed, and a reviewer should say which of those two numbers the book actually improved on.
10. **A name on anything.** He is on nothing in this band. The flour bill in a kitchen in Ashgill is the only one, and it was written in Chapter 303 by a man of sixty-three who did not know he was doing it.
11. **A Volume 06 resolution restated or improved on.** The corridor stands, the status is on a door, the column is unadded, the right of refusal is unrestored, the bell is unhung, and **none of the six is mentioned in either band of this volume and that is two bands of absence on purpose.**
12. **The midpoint reversal, which may not land in Batch 0001 or Batch 0002 and which no character in Chapters 311 to 320 may speculate and be right.** It is fixed at Chapter 321, 322, 323 or 324, and one of them must not, and none of them does.
13. **A climax that is a fight, a rescue, a relic hunt, or a monster hunt.** It is six rooms and a thing that burns on its own, and it is not written yet.
14. **A hand-off that is wrong about a character's sex or age.** **This band REMOVES one of Batch 0001's two deliberate age collisions**: the woman of about thirty-four at a gate four days up the lane does not appear in any of Chapters 311 to 320, so there is one reader risk about her and not two. Sable Venn is a woman and is in Chapter 313. The woman of forty-one in Draymoor is not the woman of forty-one at the board. The clerk of forty-four is a man and is not named.
15. **A state file that disagrees with a delivery note in the same commit, or a state file that is wrong because a figure was typed twice.** The fourth class of defect in this batch was exactly that: **six dates and distances were wrong in the prose because an interval was written from memory instead of being counted off the chapter's own day, and every one of them was caught by reading and not by a scan, and a scan for dates does not exist in this project and should.**

### WHAT THIS BAND'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, ALL OF IT CAUGHT BY THE PROJECT'S OWN INSTRUMENTS OR BY THE PROJECT'S OWN HABITS, AND NONE OF IT SURVIVING

1. **NINE WEEKDAYS ACROSS SIX CHAPTERS**, every one of them by the same habit, all cut, all replaced with a day this country can say.
2. **THE META SWEEP RETURNED FIFTEEN** across six chapters, fourteen of them the narrator stepping outside the book, and the two worst — *the reason this chapter exists* and *what that chapter said the cost would be* — were not caught by the instrument and were found by reading.
3. **THE BOLD SCAN RETURNED FOUR**, and two of them were the narrator doing the two jobs this volume forbids the narrator from doing: adjudicating that a woman with a bag is not going to be chosen, and delivering the batch as a verdict.
4. **THE STRING `cannot read` AT `319`**, in a boy's mouth about his own book, which was not a prohibited disclosure and was cut anyway.
5. **THE NUMERIC HEDGE RAN AT TWO HUNDRED AND SEVENTY-ONE, WHICH IS OVER THE TARGET**, and one hundred and eighty-six instances were cut by hand. **This is the first time in this project that a band came in over the target on the first writing, and the reason is structural: a chapter that puts six arrangements in a room is a list, and a list of things a person can be asked about is exactly where *about four people understood* lives.**
6. **A BARE-ORDINAL DAY FORM AT `316` THAT WAS WRONG** — *the sixth day of the week* in a chapter that is the third morning of the sixty-seventh week — which is the fourth form the instruments now know about.
7. **SIX INTERVALS WRITTEN FROM MEMORY** — four days instead of eleven, nine instead of fifteen, four instead of eight, four instead of ten, three instead of one, and nine days of notice instead of three weeks — and the class is the one this project has been bitten by most: a figure measured once in the prose and typed a second time has to be measured twice, and here the figure was an interval and no instrument in this project measures intervals.
8. **A DUPLICATED SENTENCE IN CHAPTER 316** after an edit, in two consecutive paragraphs, cut, and recorded because a delivery has to be read after it is instrumented and not only instrumented.
9. **AND THE FIGURES THAT WERE RIGHT THE FIRST TIME**, which is recorded because a record that only lists failures teaches a pass to expect failures: **the board chain closed on the first writing, the ten own rings sum to sixty-three, the day-count is right in all ten, the third question on the day phrases came out clean in the delivered text, the money is one table figure and four units and no product, `**` is zero, the panel count is zero, the self-arrived class is zero, the age collisions were not increased, and the third question on the six places comes out clean.**

### THE REVIEW OF THIS BAND, AND WHAT WAS DONE ABOUT IT

**A REVIEW RAN ON 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 AND ITS FULL RECEIPT, ITEM BY ITEM, IS §10 OF THE TOP BLOCK OF `state/current.md`, WHICH IS THE AUTHORITY. ITS LOG IS `logs/batch-0002.review.log`. IT FOUND FOUR P1, SIX P2 AND A STANDING P3 LIST. ALL TEN FIXABLE ITEMS ARE FIXED. NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO CHAPTER LOST A SCENE, NO FINDING WAS ADDED OR REMOVED, NO SCHEDULED CHAPTER CHANGED, AND THE PLANNED PLOT DID NOT MOVE.**

**WHAT THE REVIEW REPRODUCED CLEAN, AND THESE ARE NOT TO BE RE-RUN:** the board chain's eight auditable steps and the hand-off's 63,955 + 4 = 63,959; `**` at zero; bare digits at zero in every body; `cannot read` at zero; System and panel at zero; weekdays at zero; month-names at zero, the four hits of *march* being Briar March; italic spans at fifty; and the span scan at seventy characters, which returns 94 cross-chapter and 50 within-chapter, **144 distinct, exactly as this file recorded it, before and after the repair.**

**THE TEN FIXES, IN ONE LINE EACH. The full argument for each is at `state/current.md` §10.**

1. **P1, the burial weight contradicted itself and the chain hung on it.** `314:33`'s worked example put a burial at three and `315:67` printed one. **The cascade was declined and the table was fixed instead**: the seven weights are now named in one sentence including *a burial is one*, and the three ambiguous sixes were re-picked so none of them contains a burial — *four bells and a fire, or four bells and a debt called in, or two strangers*. **All ten of the band's own days are now derivable from that one sentence, which no chapter in either Volume 07 band could do before.**
2. **P1, the six were not a fixed set.** `311` named six arrangements, `319` named six people with the woman of thirty dropped and the barwoman substituted, and `320` then ran to seven and said *all of it came from the six*. **The woman of thirty is in, the barwoman is out, and she is out on the page**, and `320:71` no longer counts its own speaker.
3. **P1, `319:51` stated as fact what three chapters refuted.** *Wants paying* is the barwoman's characterization and is now reported as hers in the fiction and in `state/current.md` §5 and in `state/open-threads.md` item 5.
4. **P1, seven meta leaks survived while the sweep was certified clean.** All seven out. **The finding is the blind spot, not the seven: the declared pattern cannot see *these ten days*, *a band of ten*, *the whole band*, or *the chapter* as a chapter.**
5. **P2, `314:29` promised a right question and never asked it.** She now names it — *what a stroke is for* — and the weighted number is the answer to it.
6. **P2, `320:37` said *eight days ago* and it was nine.** `312:15` puts the man of sixty-three there on his sixty-first morning and Chapter 320 is the seventieth.
7. **P2, `320:11` began a sentence with a small letter.** Corrected, **and the same defect was found and fixed in the same pass at `313:15`**, which the reviewer's pattern did not name.
8. **P2, `313:15` printed the levy in the one form both money rules forbid.** The working is now in the sentence that names the figure.
9. **P2, prohibition 7 was certified against a paraphrase broader than the prohibition.** Corrected to the outline's words in three files; `311:63` stands.
10. **P2, three unreconciled measurements.** One method declared once; the hedge is reported as both eighty-eight across the files and eighty-seven in the prose with the reason; the band is 22,280 words; the cross-band figure is **294 in 45,056, one in every 153**.

**THE P3 LIST STANDS ON PURPOSE** and is at §10: `state/phase-ledger.json` still reads Batch 0002, `NOVEL_SPEC.md`'s Status is behind, `reviews/` holds Volumes 01 to 04, and **`outline/volume-07.md:156` still says Volume 07's panel register *is not fixed in this file***. **All four are controller or outline files and a writer phase may not edit them. The panel register is the one a later outline-bearing phase should fix, because twenty chapters of this volume have gone by with no interface and the register that governs them is still undefined.**

**TWO THINGS THE REPAIR FOUND THAT THE REVIEW DID NOT ASK FOR, FLAGGED RATHER THAN FIXED.** The base seam of the board chain, where four is unaccounted for between this file's predecessor and the Batch 0001 archive block, which is not edited by this phase and which touches no printed figure. And the day-phrase count, which went **up** from fourteen to sixteen because four leaks were fixed with real dates rather than deletions, which is the right repair and which obliges a check.

**AND ONE THING THAT DID NOT MOVE, WHICH IS THE ANSWER TO THE QUESTION THE BAND ASKED OF ITSELF.** The repair rewrote seven narrative sentences across six chapters and the numeric hedge moved by **zero**. The twelve counts the band is proud of are a real fall and not prose written to please an instrument.
## VOLUME 07 BATCH 0001 IS DELIVERED — Chapters 301–310, *The Seams*. **The live block. The block below it, `## VOLUME 06 IS CLOSED, AND BATCH 0005 IS DELIVERED`, and everything under that, is archive and is not edited by this phase.**

> **WHAT THIS BLOCK IS: the batch receipt, in one block, because this is the first band of Volume 07 and the state files had to carry a fact base for four more bands. What the batch paid, once each. What it did not pay. The counts. The fifteen things a later pass gets wrong if it does not measure them. And what this band's own first writing got wrong, all of it caught by the project's own instruments or by the project's own habits, and none of it surviving to the delivery.**

### WHAT THE BAND PAID, ONCE EACH

**IT PAID THE BAND'S FINDING, IN A PORCH, IN A VALLEY OF ABOUT SIXTY PEOPLE, IN THE MOUTH OF A WOMAN OF ABOUT SEVENTY-FOUR WHO HAS TOLD NOBODY FOR FORTY YEARS: a thing kept alive by one person is a thing that stops when that person stops. He cannot fix it. He does not try. He has no technique for it, there is no stage for it, and nothing in this project is going to put a person back on a list and call it a restoration. She is entitled to stop. She did not stop. Nobody thanked him. Nobody is relieved. The board has eleven names on it and nobody crossed two of them out and nobody is going to.**

**IT PAID THE SEAM, NAMED FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THE FICTION, AND IT PAID IT AS A TECHNIQUE AND NOT AS A POWER, which is the whole of Volume 07's inheritance from Volume 04: the line where one arrangement stops being true and another one starts, seen instantaneously, not a reading, cannot be written down, cannot be checked by anybody else, has never been wrong, and has never been any use to him at all until Chapter 301.** He used it six times in ten chapters and it was worthless six times, and the sixth time it was exactly right in a porch and he still stood there with nothing to do.

**IT PAID THE BATCH'S PRICE, PAID ONCE AND NOT TWICE AND NOT THROUGHOUT: about nine people in a market four days up the lane now know that he can see where two arrangements meet, and there is no instrument in eleven villages or in any town at the head of a lane that can take a knowing back.** He had told nobody for about two years, he said it out loud on a weighbridge with a fair running and about nine loads gone over it, nobody asked him to, and he was told no in about nine words by the only person in that market whose answer mattered.

**IT PAID SIX PLACES, FIXED FOR THE WHOLE OF VOLUME 07, two of them in the March and one in a market four days up in country no living person in this book has stood in and three of them off a lane, none of them a battlefield, a temple, a vault, a cave or a ruin, and not one of them produced and not one of them sent for.** The six wants are in `state/continuity.md`'s top block and in `state/character-state.md`'s top block and a reader can say every one of them without being told twice.

**IT PAID THE DISCOVERY THAT HE CANNOT BE IN TWO OF THE SIX PLACES AT ONCE, said out loud once, in his own mouth, to three people who had not asked him anything, at `307:81`, and it is the plainest sentence in the band and the one the whole of the volume is built to test.**

**IT PAID THE PARAGRAPH THAT PLANTS THE MULTI-SITE HEARING, in a man of fifty-two's mouth, in one paragraph, in Chapter 306, and not one word of it: a district under a corridor cannot be the scene of a hearing about its own standing, and so a place under a corridor has to send somebody, and the somebody has no standing either, and he has been the somebody in nine counties in nineteen years and has never once been asked whether he would go and has gone every time.** It rests there and is not returned to in Chapters 307 to 310.

**AND IT PAID A FINDING ABOUT THE AUTHORSHIP OF A SHEET, WHICH IS THE FIRST TIME IN THREE HUNDRED AND TEN CHAPTERS THAT ANYBODY HAS ASKED FOR HIS NAME IN A PLACE, and he did not ask for it: a man of sixty-three wrote *Vester, Ilyan* on the back of a flour bill in a kitchen in Ashgill because a stranger said he was going to walk a hedge.** A name at the foot of a thing is the only part of it anybody can be asked about, he has spent seven weeks telling four people that in about nine yards, and he said the price of it out loud in that kitchen and did not argue with it, and the man of sixty-three does not know what he has done and is never going to be told and the cost falls on the stranger. **THAT IS THE BAND'S THIRD PAID PRICE AND IT IS THE SAME SHAPE AS THE PRICE PAID TO THE WOMAN OF FORTY-ONE OVER A FIGURE IN PENCIL, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME A WORD HE SAID OUT LOUD HAS MADE HIM A PERSON IN A RECORD WITHOUT HIS CONSENT.**

### WHAT THE BAND DID NOT PAY, AND IT IS FOUR THINGS AND ALL FOUR ARE THE VOLUME'S INHERITANCE

1. **The right of refusal is not restored and nobody has one.** The four words at the head of the plank in the March are not reprinted in any of the ten chapters, and the right of refusal is not mentioned in any of them. **AND CHAPTER 307 ADDED A SECOND INSTANCE OF THE SAME HOLE FOUR HUNDRED YEARS ON IN A DIFFERENT COUNTY: there is no form for stopping, because stopping was never the thing anybody imagined, and the day somebody first wanted to there was not a form.**
2. **The column of a hundred and forty-one figures has not been added up and was not mentioned in any of the ten chapters, and may not be summed and what the figures count is not known to anybody.**
3. **The corridor is still standing and is not going to be torn up, and the man who signed it is alive in his own field and is owed a bell, and NEITHER IS MENTIONED IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS.** The status is on a door in a village of about ninety people and is not mentioned. The register did not gain a place and did not lose one. The bell in the March is not hung. The bell in Draymoor is not rung. Nesh Arrad's clock keeps running. The second notice stays unpaid. The dead man in the fourth line stays dead. The eleven feet stay off the settlement. The charter on the wall in Sallowby is revocable and is not amended. **AND THE WHOLE OF THAT LIST IS ABSENCE AND NOT ACCIDENT, AND IT IS THE FIFTH BAND RUNNING IN WHICH A WHOLE VOLUME'S STANDING COSTS HAVE NOT BEEN BROUGHT INTO A CHAPTER FOR ATMOSPHERE, AND A LATER WRITER WHO DOES IT UNDOES FIFTY CHAPTERS OF NOT DOING IT.**
4. **He did not become a better man.** He is worse off than when the band opened: he is a name on a bill, nine people know a thing about him, the market has a version of it that is larger than what he said and cannot be corrected, and he has a date he did not write down. **And the one thing he has got better at, and the chapter says so in its last line, is that he has never once known what he was going to say to anybody.**

### THE FIFTEEN THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS BOUGHT BY A FAILURE THIS PROJECT HAD

1. **The board chain, and the ten own rings on a line of their own.** The morning figure in row *N* is row *N-1* plus the rings in row *N*, and the rings in a row are the PREVIOUS CHAPTER'S OWN DAY. The ten morning figures are 63,849 / 63,853 / 63,856 / 63,863 / 63,865 / 63,873 / 63,874 / 63,878 / 63,881 / 63,888. **THE SEVEN PRINTED IN THE FICTION ARE THE FIRST FOUR, THEN 63,874, 63,878 and 63,888; 63,865, 63,873 and 63,881 ARE NOT PRINTED, AND CHAPTERS 305, 306 AND 309 SAY IN THE TEXT WHY.** The ten own-days are 4 / 3 / 7 / 2 / 8 / 1 / 4 / 3 / 7 / 8 and they sum to forty-seven and 63,841 + 47 = 63,888. **Chapter 310's own day of eight produces 63,896 and is not printed and belongs to Batch 0002.**
2. **The chapter's-own-day rule, and Chapter 251 being the first.** Chapter 301 is the fifty-first morning and Chapter 310 is the sixtieth, and the prose says all ten in words and prints none in digits. **And Chapter 310 is a round number, and the correct treatment is the one delivered: noticed out loud in the first three hundred words, refused as evidence in about nine words, no coincidence built on it, and the chapter does not return to it.**
3. **THE BATCH 0001 PROMPT'S OWN TABLE IS NOT AUTHORITATIVE ON THREE OF ITS TEN ROWS, AND THE DELIVERY TOOK THE MORNING FIGURES AND THE OWN-RINGS LINE AND WROTE THE CARDS' EVENTS AND PRINTED NO DAY'S OWN TOTAL ANYWHERE.** Three of the ten totals the prompt fixes are 1, 2 and 3, and a day's figure in the March is four bells plus a weight and the weights are 3, 4, 7, 2, 1 and 2, so a total of 1, 2 or 3 is not constructible from this country's own arithmetic; and the card prose in the same prompt produces a different total on seven of the ten days. **A LATER WRITER MUST NOT 'FIX' THIS BY ADDING A TOTAL TO A CHAPTER. THE MORNING FIGURES AND THE OWN-RINGS LINE CLOSE EXACTLY AND THE DAY'S OWN TOTAL IS THE ONE FIGURE IN THIS BAND THAT IS BETTER LEFT TO THE PLANK, WHICH IS THE BAND'S OWN INHERITED FINDING.**
4. **The day-phrase sweep, widened to three alternatives and declared.** The two-alternative pattern declared in the prompt returns three over this band; the prose says its day in a third form, *the second morning of the sixty-fifth week*, which neither alternative matches; the three-alternative pattern returns thirteen. **A later pass that runs only the declared two will see almost nothing and must not report that as a clean band.**
5. **The span scan at seventy and at forty, with the unit and the threshold and the range declared, and the range is SIXTY files on disk and not thirty.** Re-measured at the review of Batch 0001, both ranges, from the files: at seventy, Chapters 241 to 300 give 1,933 distinct duplicated windows in 3,958 occurrences, this batch gives 235 in 533, and the two together give 2,168 in 4,491; at forty, 11,879 in 26,945, this batch 876 in 1,979, and the two together 12,755 in 28,924. **The figures this item used to print did not reproduce under the declared method and the two 'over the two together' sums did not add up, and both are replaced.** **The within-batch residual at seventy is 194 cross-chapter windows in exactly two declared classes — the man of sixty-three's nine words at `302:91` and `310:77`, 155 windows, a MOTIF — and the board-chain opening construction at six chapter openings, 39 windows, MANDATED BY THE PROMPT — and 41 within-chapter windows which are the two money workings stated twice on purpose, and a later pass that re-words a printed working has broken the rule that produced it.**
6. **The bold scan must return ZERO and the panel count must return ZERO.** Volume 07's panel register is not fixed in `outline/volume-07.md:156` and this band spent none of it; a later band may spend one only if that file is fixed in the same breath.
7. **Every money figure is in the volume outline's table or a difference between two rows of it, and there are exactly two named ones and one refusal to print a product, and the unit price must be found in the sentence and multiplied.** This band's own first writing had four wrong money figures and every one of them was well formed and every one of them had the unit price in the sentence.
8. **Do not name a weekday, a month-name, a position beneath anything, the First Witness, or a vault, a cave, a ruin, a temple or a battlefield.** The first writing of this band had a weekday and a month-name and both were caught.
9. **Do not print a count of anybody who knows anything about Ilyan, in any file, for any reason, including a hand-off, and let no character remark on the asking.** Refer to `154:53` and `164:49` and to nothing else. The string *cannot read* is at zero.
10. **Do not put his name on anything, and do not fill or dash anything — with exactly one exception in this band, and it is a name he did not ask for, written by a person of a place who is not to be told, and the cost of it falls on him.** A later pass must not accidentally undo it and must not build on it either.
11. **Do not let a Volume 06 resolution be asserted as still open, restated or improved on.** The corridor stands, the status is on a door, the column is unadded, the right of refusal is unrestored, both bells are owed and neither is rung, and NONE OF THE SIX IS MENTIONED IN CHAPTERS 301 TO 310.
12. **Do not let the midpoint reversal land in Batch 0001 or Batch 0002, and do not let a character in Chapters 301 to 320 speculate it and be right.** Nobody in this band guessed it and no character says the word a witness about anything.
13. **Do not let the climax be a fight, a rescue, a relic hunt or a monster hunt.** It is six rooms and a thing that burns on its own, and it is not written yet.
14. **Do not let a hand-off be wrong about a character's sex or age, and there are TWO DELIBERATE AGE COLLISIONS IN THIS BAND AND ONE THAT WAS A DEFECT AND HAS BEEN FIXED: the woman of about fifty-one in a kitchen at Ashgill is not Isel Cardew of Trope, and the woman of about thirty-four at a gate four days up the lane is not the woman of about thirty-four at the bar eleven miles down and off it. THE THIRD WAS NOT A COLLISION BUT A COLLAPSE, and a review of Batch 0001 found it: the opening of Chapter 308 had a single sixty-word sentence holding TWO women both called about thirty-four, and it hung the board with four letters and a stroke at the top of it on the wrong one of them, and that board belongs to the woman of about FIFTY at the bar at the bottom end of the lane, and the same chapter then put its own bar at the end of this lane in one line and at the head of the lane in two others with the tenth village eleven miles down and off it. The sentence is rebuilt, the gate-woman has no board, the barwoman's board is her own, and the tenth village's bar is the bar of this village and is not the bar at the head of the lane. Sable Venn is a woman and does not appear. The clerk of forty-four is a man and is not named. The woman of forty-one in Draymoor is not the woman of forty-one at the board. **AND A FOURTH THING THIS ITEM HAS TO CARRY, WHICH IS NOT AN AGE: until the review of Batch 0001 the Coldharrow figure had TWO CARRIERS IN TEN CHAPTERS. Six chapter openings have the woman of forty-one writing it on the plank at the second light and the man of about thirty-eight carrying or repeating it, and Chapter 310 had invented a second board-keeper at Coldharrow, a woman of about fifty-one writing on the back of a bill and a boy running it eleven miles, and a woman of about fifty-one in this band is a person in a kitchen at Ashgill and a person who cannot write four hundred miles off, and there is no third. THE FIGURE HAS ONE WRITER AND ONE CARRIER IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS AND A LATER WRITER MUST NOT ADD A THIRD** |
15. **Do not prune the state files inside a phase that is also writing prose, do not edit the archive blocks below the top block of any of the six, and do not let a delivery note and a state file disagree about a figure in the same commit.** This block and the top block of `state/current.md` were both written from the chapter files and neither was written from the prompt, which is the only reason they disagree with the prompt's tables at all, and that disagreement is recorded in both places and not resolved in either.

### WHAT THIS BAND'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, ALL OF IT CAUGHT BY THE PROJECT'S OWN INSTRUMENTS OR BY THE PROJECT'S OWN HABITS, AND NONE OF IT SURVIVED

- **A WEEKDAY, *Thursday*, at `308:85`**, in a man's mouth in a room over a smithy, put there by a habit that reached for a week-day name the way this country reaches for a day. Caught by the plural-safe scan and cut.
- **A MONTH-NAME, *April*, at `305:45`**, in a woman's mouth: *I am going to be stood up here in April instead of doing the sums.* Caught by the month-word scan after the modal verb *may* was subtracted, and cut and reworded to *at the spring*. **THE SUBTRACTION IS THE POINT: THE RAW COUNT IS TEN AND NINE OF THEM ARE *MAY* AND ONE IS THE REGION, AND WITHOUT THE SUBTRACTION A WRITER BELIEVES THREE MONTH-NAMES WHEN THERE ARE NONE AND A REVIEWER BELIEVES NONE WHEN THERE IS ONE.**
- **THE MONEY WAS WRONG IN FIVE PLACES, ALL FIVE WELL FORMED, ALL FIVE WITH THE UNIT PRICE IN THE SENTENCE — AND THE FIFTH WAS FOUND BY THE REVIEW OF BATCH 0001, NOT BY THE WRITER'S OWN PASS, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS MOST.** In Chapter 303 the metal figure was printed as *one thousand four hundred and forty-eight* where thirty-one pounds at four shillings a pound is **1,488**, and **THE ADDITION WAS NEVER THE BROKEN PART** — 1,488 + 156 is **1,644** and 1,488 + 132 is **1,620** and both totals were correct on the page in every writing — and the defect was entirely in the term being added, which is invisible to any check that reads a sum and does not multiply. And 384 pence was printed as *one pound and four*, and 384 pence is thirty-two shillings and thirty-two shillings is **one pound and twelve**, and that wrong conversion was copied into this file as well, so one measurement had been typed twice and agreed with itself. In Chapter 308, three thousand seven hundred and eighty pence was printed as *three thousand six hundred and eighty pence of a pound and a hundred pence over*, and the remainder above £15 is **a hundred and eighty pence**, which is fifteen shillings to the penny. **THIS IS THE FIFTH TIME THE UNIT-PRICE INSTRUMENT HAS CAUGHT ONE AND THE THIRD TIME IT HAS CAUGHT A MALFORMED SUM RATHER THAN A MALFORMED UNIT, AND THE CHAPTERS ARE BETTER FOR IT: A WRONG FIGURE IN A ROOM WITH NO CLERK IN IT STAYS IN THE ROOM FOREVER, AND THAT IS WHAT THE MAN OF THIRTY-EIGHT SAYS OUT LOUD WHEN HE ADMITS IT.**
- **THE META SWEEP RETURNED EIGHT**, seven of them the narrator saying *this project*, *this chapter* and *this volume* out loud, and one the literal string *in a book in* at `308:77` as an accident of a phrase. All eight are out and the one that survives is *this stretch*, meaning a length of hedge.
- **A REFRAIN CARRIED ACROSS A MOUTH AT `303:49`**, where a man of thirty-one built the woman of forty-one's own construction in his own mouth, and it was cut.
- **THE STRING `cannot read` AT `307:49`**, in a boy's mouth about a burying ground, which was not a prohibited disclosure and was cut anyway, because the string is an instrument and a string that means one thing in a chapter means another thing in a review.
- **THE DAY-PHRASE SWEEP COULD NOT SEE TWO THIRDS OF THE BAND AND THE TWO IT COULD NOT SEE WERE BOTH WRONG.** `304:13` named the sixth day of the fifty-eighth week for a doorway that is the fourth day of that week, and `310:75` named the third morning of the sixty-fifth week for a field that is the fourth morning of that week. Both are the band's own prose, both were found by hand, and neither was found by the sweep, which is the tenth time the third question has beaten the legality sweep and the second time the sweep's own blind spot was the defect.
- **THE SEVENTY-CHARACTER SPAN SCAN RETURNED NINETY-FOUR** within the batch and one rewordable source was found by reading the clusters and cut; the residual is the two printed money workings and is declared.
- **AND THE FIGURES THAT WERE RIGHT THE FIRST TIME, which is recorded because a record that only lists failures teaches a pass to expect failures: the board chain closed on the first writing, all seven morning figures are in the right chapters and in words, the ten own rings check to forty-seven, the day-count is right in all ten, the seam's six uses and the money's two named figures are where the cards put them, `**` is zero, the panel count is zero, the numeric hedge came in under target without being hunted, and the third question on the six places is clean.** Ten of the twelve defect classes this project has been bitten by in three hundred and ten chapters did not happen here, and the two that did are the money and the calendar, and both were caught by instruments that were built after being bitten, which is the argument for building them.

### AND WHAT A REVIEWER SHOULD ATTACK FIRST, IN THIS ORDER, AND THE THING THIS PHASE CANNOT DO ITSELF

1. **The board chain against the top block of `state/current.md` §1, morning by morning, and then the prompt's own table against it, because §7.2 there says the prompt is wrong on three rows and a reviewer should confirm that independently rather than take it.**
2. **The unit-price instrument on the two named figures, sentence by sentence, not as a list, and then on the four figures the first writing got wrong, which are the ones a reviewer can reconstruct from the state file alone.**
3. **The day-phrase sweep widened to three alternatives, and then the third question on all thirteen references by hand.**
4. **The six places and the six wants, asked of the reader, and the two deliberate age collisions.**
5. **The seam at `310:63`–`310:71`, and whether the chapter lets him win with it.**
6. **The list of what the band did not do: the right of refusal is restored nowhere, the column is added up nowhere, the corridor is not torn up, the bell is not hung, the midpoint has not landed, nothing was put beside anything, no document was produced, and nobody is relieved, forgiven, redeemed or thanked anywhere in ten chapters.**

**AND ONE DEFECT IN THE BATCH 0002 PROMPT ITSELF, FOUND BY RUNNING ITS OWN TABLE BEFORE WRITING A WORD OF IT AGAINST IT, AND RECORDED HERE BECAUSE IT IS THE FIFTH TIME THIS PROJECT HAS TYPED A RING COLUMN WITHOUT CHECKING WHICH CHAPTER IT BELONGS TO. THE FIRST WRITING OF `workspace/volume-07/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`'s BOARD TABLE HAD THE RINGS COLUMN SHIFTED ONE ROW LEFT ON ITS LAST THREE ROWS — 318 carried 4 where it carries 10, 319 carried 7 where it carries 4, and 320 carried 10 where it carries 7 — WHICH PUT THE MORNING FIGURES FOR THREE CHAPTERS OUT BY NINE, THREE AND THREE. THE CHAIN DID NOT CLOSE. IT WAS CAUGHT BY RUNNING THE FILE'S OWN CELLS BACK THROUGH THE ARITHMETIC AND COMPARING THEM, WHICH IS THE ONLY THING THAT CATCHES IT, AND THE TABLE NOW READS 8, 4, 7, 8, 6, 5, 8, 10, 4, 7 AGAINST 63,896 / 63,900 / 63,907 / 63,915 / 63,921 / 63,926 / 63,934 / 63,944 / 63,948 / 63,955 AND THE CHAIN CLOSES AND ALL TEN TOTALS ARE CONSTRUCTIBLE FROM THE MARCH'S OWN WEIGHTS, WHICH IS WHAT BATCH 0001'S PROMPT WAS NOT.**

**A REVIEW IS REQUIRED AND HAS NOT BEEN RUN. NOTHING IN THIS BLOCK IS A REVIEW.**

### AND WHAT THE NEXT PHASE IS, MEASURED AND NOT BELIEVED

`ls workspace/volume-07/` returns one directory, `batch-0001/`, and this phase added exactly one, `batch-0002/`, holding one file, `PROMPT.md`, for Chapters 311 to 320, written against the Volume 07 section of `outline/series.md`, against `outline/volume-07.md`, and against the new top block of `state/current.md`. **There is no volume-close prompt for Volume 07, because Volume 07 is fifty chapters long and this is its first of five bands, and there is no `.done` marker and no `.complete` marker and no ledger and no phase file, because those are the controller's and not this phase's.**

## VOLUME 06 IS CLOSED, AND BATCH 0005 IS DELIVERED — Chapters 291–300, *The Erasure, And The Status*. **The live block. The block below it, from `## VOLUME 06 BATCH 0004 IS DELIVERED` down, is archive and is not edited by this phase.**

> **WHAT THIS BLOCK IS: the volume's receipt, in one block, because this is the last band of Volume 06 and there is no fifth batch and no volume-close phase. What the five batches each paid, once each. What the volume as a whole paid. The two things it did not pay. The list of the things a later pass gets wrong if it does not measure them, fifteen of them, and every one of them bought by a failure this project had already. And what this band's own first writing got wrong, all of it caught by the project's own instruments or by the project's own habits, and none of it surviving to the delivery.**

### WHAT THIS BAND PAID, ONCE EACH

**IT PAID THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX AND IT PAID IT AS CIVIC WORK IN A ROOM, WHICH IS WHAT `outline/series.md` ASKED FOR AND NOT AS A FIGHT, A RESCUE OR A MONSTER HUNT.** A city put a district of about a hundred and forty people off every map it keeps and called the place a corridor, and a corridor is a legal instrument and a legal instrument is a document and a document can be produced, in a place, on a day, to a person, and it works, and it is the fastest and the cheapest thing available, and about nine of the hundred and forty are children, and nobody in the room can say what it is for and the city is not required to say. **A leader of forty accepted an abusive bargain out loud, in his own mouth, before anybody asked him, with the working printed, and everybody heard it and nobody could stop him and the chapter did not stop him.** Ilyan defended the district for about nine minutes of ordinary civic work and it did not work, and he got one sentence out of the room, and he could not undo the bargain and did not try. **THE BAND'S CENTRE IS ONE SENTENCE REFUSED: a man of thirty-one carried a nine-word sentence for fifty days and offered it in a room, and a woman of forty-one told him that a word is not a person.**

**IT PAID THE VOLUME'S RESOLUTION, VERBATIM FROM `outline/series.md`, AND BOTH HALVES OF IT.** Null communities gain recognized civic status — one sheet, short, bounded, recognized, written, on the inside of a door, with no gate, no toll, no bar, no date, no bench, no seat, no chair, no room with a door on it except a press, no vote, no meeting, no officer and no money, held by the eleven villages and about forty others and kept by a recordwright of twenty-eight who is not in it. And Sable Venn loses sole authority over the movement — said by a person of a village, in a village, in about ten words, unasked, and she agreed before it had finished, and she keeps about thirty people of her own, and she is not relieved and not thanked and not rescued and not redeemed and not the villain of it and did not lose, and no chapter of the band is about her and nothing was taken off her by him.

**AND IT PAID THE FINDING THAT THE WHOLE FIFTY CHAPTERS WERE FOR, in an ordinary mouth, about nine words, standing up in a yard: a status with no day in it is not a lesser thing than a corridor with a day in it, it is the same paper with the day left out on purpose, and that is the whole of what four hundred years of a list on the inside of a door was for.**

### WHAT THE BAND DID NOT PAY, AND IT IS THREE THINGS AND ALL THREE ARE THE VOLUME'S INHERITANCE

1. **The bargain is still standing.** It was not torn up, nobody tried, there is no seam in it and nothing on the back of it and nothing to appeal to, and the bell it bought is owed and unpaid and nobody bought it, and the price of a bell, hung, with a rope, is six pounds fifteen against a keeper's year at five pounds five, and the difference is one pound ten every year for as long as it is rung. **The man who signed it is alive and in his own field and was not relieved and not forgiven and not thanked and is owed a bell.**
2. **The right of refusal is not restored and nobody has one.** The four words at the head of the plank are not reprinted in any of the ten chapters, and a woman of fifty-one said it out loud in a yard in about nine words: nobody in this lane can refuse one single thing today that they could not refuse seven weeks ago, and nobody in that valley could refuse the corridor either.
3. **The column of a hundred and forty-one figures has not been added up and was refused a third time out loud, on purpose, in the last chapter of the volume**, and what the figures count is not known to the man of thirty-one or to a recordwright of twenty-eight or to a clerk of forty-four four hundred miles off, and no room in any county has ever asked, and a later volume may not answer it and may not sum it.

### THE FIFTEEN THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS BOUGHT BY A FAILURE THIS PROJECT HAD

1. **The board chain, and the off-by-one between the two columns of the outline's Batch 0005 table.** The morning figure in row *N* is row *N-1* plus the rings in row *N*, which means the rings in a row are the PREVIOUS CHAPTER'S OWN DAY. The ten morning figures are 63,778 / 63,782 / 63,786 / 63,799 / 63,807 / 63,813 / 63,817 / 63,825 / 63,833 / 63,841 and the ten own-days are 4 / 4 / 13 / 8 / 6 / 4 / 8 / 8 / 8 / 8, and the last morning in the volume is 63,841 and the first morning after it is 63,849 and is in no table on purpose.
2. **The chapter's-own-day rule, and the day in Chapter 297.** 291 is the forty-first day and 300 is the fiftieth, and the Chapter 296 card said forty-one where the chapter says forty-seven.
3. **The span scan at seventy and at forty, and the range is FIFTY files on disk and not thirty.** Four hundred and forty-four at seventy against the previous band, two hundred and fifty-seven within the batch, and the residual is declared in `state/current.md` §2.4 and §2.5 and is mostly the two printed money workings.
4. **The bold scan must return FOUR marks and all four are the two panel lines in Chapter 294.** Eighty-six italic spans and no limit on them.
5. **The chalk count is FIVE for the whole volume and all five are in Batch 0001.** This batch added none and the chalk stood for forty-nine days at the last morning.
6. **A count of anybody who knows anything about Ilyan is printed in no file for any reason, and the string *cannot read* occurs ZERO times in this band.** Refer to `154:53` and `164:49` and to nothing else.
7. **Every money figure is in the outline's table and the ONE figure the band derived is the difference between a bell and a keeper's year, and the unit price must be found in the sentence and multiplied.** The first writing printed a carter's four days' walking as a hundred and twenty pence, which is the REMAINDER of a carter's year and is not four times eighteen.
8. **Ask the third question no regex can ask, and widen the pattern before you trust the sweep.** The sweep returned twenty-three day-references, all legal, and a reader found two defects it certified: an inherited wrong back-reference at `290:29` in the previous chapter, and Chapter 294's own day at `294:5`, which said the fourth day of the sixty-fourth week where the chapter is the second and the fourth belongs to `296:3`. **The pattern required the literal `day of the` and the chapter used the bare form, so the instrument could not see the one reference in the band that was wrong. A sweep that matches one way of saying a thing certifies nothing about the other ways.**
9. **Do not let an instrument open a chapter file for writing.** A repair script over seven of the ten files in this band normalised their whitespace and destroyed their paragraph structure, and the seven were rewritten from the author's own text.
10. **The panel register is three and all three are spent, and there are two non-panel traces and two is the whole of them.**
11. **Do not name a weekday, a month-name, an anchor, a stone, the First Witness, or a position beneath anything, and do not use *stone* for masonry.** The first writing used *Tuesday* once in a character's mouth.
12. **Do not let a hand-off be wrong about a character's sex or age.** Corvin Hale is a man of forty. The woman of forty-one in Draymoor is a different person. The man of thirty-one who owns the tower at Nettlebed is never to be confused with the man of thirty-one from four hundred miles off. Sable Venn is a woman. The clerk of forty-four is a man and is not named in any chapter of Volume 06.
13. **Do not prune the state files inside a phase that is also writing prose, and do not edit the archive blocks below the top block of any of the six.**
14. **Do not let a later volume restage the bargain or the corridor's document.** Both are spent devices and the difference between them is written down at `state/current.md` §6.
15. **The thing the column counts is still unknown, nobody may answer it, nobody may sum it, and a writer who needs the number must go and ask a person of a village.**

### WHAT THIS BAND'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, ALL OF IT CAUGHT BY THE PROJECT'S OWN INSTRUMENTS OR BY THE PROJECT'S OWN HABITS, AND NONE OF IT SURVIVED

**AND THE FIRST IS THE WORST AND NO INSTRUMENT IN THIS PROJECT FOUND IT.** A repair script run over seven of the ten files normalised their whitespace and wrote the result back, and it destroyed the paragraph structure of Chapters 293, 294, 295, 297, 298, 299 and 300. Those seven files were rewritten from the author's own text with the paragraph breaks and the scene breaks restored and every figure and every subsequent edit carried across, and **the rule that comes out of it is not the seven files: the duplicate-span instrument in this project REQUIRES A REWRITE, and no instrument in this repository may open a chapter file for writing.**

- **A WEEKDAY, *Tuesday*, in a character's mouth in Chapter 295**, caught by the plural-safe scan and cut.
- **FOUR BOLD MARKS IN CHAPTER 297, a chapter that spent no panel**, caught by the bold scan and changed to italics.
- **THE PANEL IN CHAPTER 294 SAID *MAY BE PRODUCED* in capitals**, which the month-word scan returns as a capital-M hit, and the line was reworded.
- **THE META SWEEP RETURNED SEVENTEEN**, fifteen of them the narrator saying *this volume* and *this chapter*, and two of them the literal string *in a book in* as an accident of the phrase *a line about it in a book in a building*, and all seventeen are out.
- **A CARTER'S FOUR DAYS' WALKING PRINTED AS A HUNDRED AND TWENTY PENCE AND AS EIGHT SHILLINGS**, both wrong, caught by the unit-price instrument because the unit price was in the sentence and four times eighteen is not a hundred and twenty, and the true figure is seventy-two pence, which is six shillings.
- **THE CROSS-BATCH SPAN SCAN AT SEVENTY RETURNED ONE THOUSAND FOUR HUNDRED AND TWENTY-ONE and the within-batch scan returned SIX HUNDRED AND FIFTY-EIGHT**, both cut to four hundred and forty-four and two hundred and fifty-seven by rewording eleven sources, and the residual is declared and not hidden.

**AND THE FIGURES THAT WERE RIGHT THE FIRST TIME, recorded because a record that only lists failures teaches a pass to expect failures: the board chain closed on the first writing, all ten morning figures, all ten own-days, all ten day-counts, the difference between a bell and a keeper's year, the three hundred and thirty-one, the sixty-three thousand eight hundred and forty-nine, the ten distinct frames for the board figure, and the whole of the three instruments the outline named for this band. Nine of the eleven defect classes this project has been bitten by in two hundred and fifty chapters did not happen here, and the one that did happen is the one nobody had an instrument for. **AND THE ONE CLAIM ON THIS LIST THAT WAS NOT RIGHT IS *ALL TWENTY-TWO DAY-PHRASES*, WHICH WAS WRONG TWICE: there are twenty-three, because the pattern could not see the bare form at `294:5`, and the reference it could not see named the wrong day of the week.**

**AND FIVE MORE, FOUND AFTER THIS RECEIPT WAS WRITTEN — TWO BY THE CLOSING SWEEP AND THREE BY THE REVIEW OF THE DELIVERY — WHICH IS WHAT A CLOSING SWEEP AND A REVIEW ARE FOR. The first two are this project's own housekeeping; the last three are the band.**

1. **A CHARACTER'S AGE, at `300:79`, and no instrument in this project could have found it.** The woman of forty-one at the board was given as *fifty-nine*, and the figure is a bleed-in from a different woman in a different chapter, a woman of about fifty-nine at `253:65` who says she is fifty-nine when this is finished being her job. The woman on the ladder at Coldharrow is forty-one and has been since Chapter 251, and every other mention of her age in fifty chapters says forty-one. Now *she is forty-one and she has about as many years of it as a bell has of rope*. **AN AGE IS A CLAIM THAT A PERSON IS SOMEBODY ELSE, and it is the only defect class in this project's list that damages a person rather than a total, and the only thing that touches it is a hand-off that lists a cast by age and a reader who reads it.**
2. **THREE ROWS OF `outline/volume-06.md`'S BATCH 0005 TABLE CARRIED A RIGHT FIGURE OVER A LABEL NAMING THE WRONG DAY.** The rings column is one row ahead of the morning column, so a row's label describes the PREVIOUS chapter's own day, and rows 298, 299 and 300 had been given a label belonging to a different day — a market, a boundary walked that is on no day in this batch at all, and a stranger respectively. All three corrected in place against `297:3`, `298:3` and `299:3`; the second table in that file was already right and the two now agree; every morning figure and the running line were never wrong. **AND THE SWEEP'S OWN FIRST ATTEMPT NAMED THE WRONG ROW AND CORRECTED IT WITH THE WRONG CHAPTER'S DAY, which is the error it was correcting, and that is recorded because a correction that does not ask which chapter a label is naming is not a correction.**
3. **`294:5` NAMED THE WRONG DAY OF THE WRONG CHAPTER, and the instrument could not see it.** It read *The forty-fourth morning of his being in that county was the fourth of the sixty-fourth week*; Chapter 294 is the forty-fourth morning and therefore the second day of that week, and the fourth is Chapter 296's morning, which `296:3` states. **The day-phrase pattern required the literal `day of the` and this sentence carries its day in the bare form, so the sweep returned twenty-two legal references and certified the only wrong one in the band.** Now *the second of the sixty-fourth week*; the pattern runs both forms; the count is twenty-three. **A LEGALITY ANSWER IS NOT A TRUTH ANSWER, AND AN INSTRUMENT THAT CANNOT SEE A FORM CANNOT CLEAR IT — and this is the ninth time the third question has beaten the legality sweep and the first time the sweep's own blind spot is the defect.**
4. **`300:13` PRINTED A FIGURE ABOUT ITS OWN VOLUME'S LAST TEN MORNINGS AND IT WAS WRONG.** It said *there are now four days in the last ten that say the same figure*; the ten own-days are 4 / 4 / 13 / 8 / 6 / 4 / 8 / 8 / 8 / 8, so eight occurs on FIVE of them and four on THREE, and neither is four. **The day-count column here had it right and the chapter had it wrong, which is the same shape as item 2 and the same shape as the first finding in this file: the tables were built from the cards and the prose was never reconciled against them.** It now reads that the last four mornings in a row have all come to eight, that two of them were market days, and that no way on earth exists to tell from a plank which of the four was a stranger and a burial — true of `297:3`, `298:3`, `299:3` and `300:77`.
5. **AND THE DELIVERY NOTE IN `outline/volume-06.md` WAS PRINTING THE TALLY THIS FILE HAD ALREADY CORRECTED, IN THE SAME COMMIT.** The bare *would like* count was corrected here from NINE to FOUR, and the note still printed NINE, still concluded the tic had gone up, and carried four stale locations of which one attributed a line to Chapter 297 that is in Chapter 298. **A note that defines a wrong label over a right figure as the worst of the three defects, in its own paragraph, and then prints one is worse than a note that has never heard of the class, because the class is on the record as settled and a later writer will trust the settling.** The note now carries this file's figures and line numbers and the two agree.
6. **AND THE ONE THING THE REVIEW MEASURED THAT NOBODY HAD BEEN MEASURING, WHICH IS NOT A DEFECT OF FACT AND IS THE LARGEST CRAFT HABIT IN THE VOLUME: the numeric hedge, *about* plus a number word, THREE HUNDRED AND THIRTY-FIVE times over the ten chapters, one in every eighty-two words, and the word *about* on its own 529 times, against four refrains totalling forty-nine.** The project tracked the two small ones band over band for five bands and did not track this. The prose is not rewritten in a repair pass and the reason is recorded: a rhythm is fixed by a volume's habit, and rewriting a hundred and forty finished sentences to move a number is the same class of mistake as the script that destroyed seven files. What is done is the measurement, which is what was missing, and a target of under two hundred and twenty-five for Volume 07's first band, which `outline/volume-07.md` prohibition 15 and the Volume 07 Batch 0001 prompt's instrument 14 now both carry with the pattern beside them.

### AND WHAT THE NEXT PHASE IS, MEASURED AND NOT BELIEVED

`ls workspace/` at the end of this phase returns one directory for Volume 07 and it is `workspace/volume-07/batch-0001/`, holding one file, `PROMPT.md`, for Chapters 301 to 310, written against the Volume 07 section of `outline/series.md` and against an `outline/volume-07.md` that did not exist and that this phase had to plan, because **Volume 07 is *The Choir of Nine Anchors* and its cards are not on disk.** There is no volume-close prompt for Volume 06, because Volume 06 is closed by this phase and the next thing is a different volume with a different central pressure, a different power progression and a different set of instruments.

## VOLUME 06 BATCH 0004 IS DELIVERED — Chapters 281–290, *The Network*. **The live block. The block below it, from `## VOLUME 06 BATCH 0003 IS DELIVERED` down, is archive and is not edited by this phase.**

> **WHAT THIS BLOCK IS: the batch's receipt, in four parts — what it paid once each, what it did not pay, the fifteen things a later pass gets wrong if it does not measure them, and what its own first writing got wrong, all of it caught by the project's own instruments.**

### WHAT THE BATCH PAID, ONCE EACH, AND NOT REPEATED

1. **THE FOUR DISPUTES, AND NONE OF THEM IS A BORDER.** A bar, a lane, a list of a hundred and forty-one names, and ninety acres, in that order, on the fifth of nine sheets cut out of the back of a bill, with nothing at the top of it.
2. **THE GATE DISPUTE CLOSED, and it closed by nobody's hand,** because a man with a barrow walked the length of the lane and found that there is no road at the far end of it, and a lane does not have a gate, and four people had been arguing about a piece of wood across a gap in a hedge for about a year. **The carter of thirty-eight did not say the answer in a yard and nobody asked him.**
3. **THE TECHNIQUE USED THREE TIMES AND THREE PRICES PAID, and the third price is the batch's finding about his own method: the only way a thing gets into it is that somebody says it out loud in front of people, and there is no instrument in eleven villages that lets anybody keep a thing to themselves.**
4. **THE METHOD'S OWN LIMIT, SAID OUT LOUD IN A YARD IN ABOUT NINE WORDS BY THE MAN WHO INVENTED IT, and it is not an answer to anything and it is not a stage: you can put them next to each other and you cannot make them agree, and a method that cannot make four villages agree is a method of evidence and is not a method of government.**
5. **A SEVEN IS NOT A SIGNATURE, and a LOAD HAS NO UNIT,** and both are the same finding about a county that counts everything and names nothing.
6. **A RECORDWRIGHT OF TWENTY-EIGHT IS THE ONLY COPY OF A THING, for the first time in six years,** and the protection that fails is not a document and not a room: it is that a record is the words and the hand and the day and the person who can be asked, and the way somebody said it does not survive being written down, and in this county the way was the whole of it. **She called it the worst record of her life and made it anyway.**
7. **THE ELEVEN FAMILIES WERE TOLD, AT LAST, IN FOUR MINUTES, BY A WOMAN WHO CANNOT NAME A DAY, and they went into a lane with nothing to be right about, exactly as Batch 0002 predicted in the fiction, and the reason it happened is that a rule in a register made a person of a village say a thing out loud that she would not otherwise have said.**
8. **A REGISTER USED, AND USING IT IS WHAT BREAKS IT, and nobody is a villain: a sentence went in that is a dispute and not an entry, a recordwright cannot tell which she has done, and the reason is the rule she wrote herself, which tells her not to write who said it.**
9. **A REQUEST TO COME OUT, which the rules do not allow, and a recordwright who wrote down the asking.** There is no twelfth line and there is not going to be one.
10. **THE MOVEMENT'S SHAPE CONTESTED FROM INSIDE IT BY A PERSON OF A VILLAGE, IN A MARKET YARD, IN ABOUT ELEVEN WORDS,** and the woman agreed before she was asked, is not relieved, is not thanked, is not defeated, is not the villain of it, keeps an honest independent following, and no chapter of this batch made the contest about her or had him take anything off her.
11. **THE REGISTER GOT SMALLER, BY TWO CROSSES, WITHOUT ANYBODY ASKING,** and a cross is not an entry and comes off the moment anybody in that place says anything at all.
12. **ONE VILLAGE SAID ITS SENTENCE AND TWO WOULD NOT, and the difference is not willingness and it is not a bell,** and three answers to what one entry is worth were printed with the working and all three are correct.
13. **NINE FORMS ENTERED WITHOUT A DAY IN NINE YEARS, and nobody in a building with a yard has ever been asked to look at what a form does after it is entered,** and the word over the column they sit in is two syllables and it came into a lane that has never had a building in it.
14. **A THING WRITTEN ON THE BACK OF A MAN OF THIRTY-FOUR'S HAND, which is the only thing he has written down in about eleven years, and a woman at a bar asked the only second question anybody has asked her in twenty-two years, and neither of them acted on anything.**
15. **THE ONE SENTENCE HE HAS THE STANDING TO SAY, NOT SAID, in a yard, in front of about nine people, with a woman of about sixty nine feet away who it would have moved.** That is the batch's climax and it is a silence and it is the volume's turn.

### WHAT THE BATCH DID NOT PAY, AND IT IS THE WHOLE OF WHAT BATCH 0005 IS FOR

**It did not pay the volume's climax, which is at Chapter 294 or 295.** There is no city in these ten chapters, no district off a map, no corridor, no document that can be produced to end a district's existence, no bargain accepted out loud with the working printed, and no bell bought. **The resolution at 299 and 300 is not paid either: nobody in these ten chapters has recognized civic status for anything, and the one place that says a thing is going to be a lane is a book on a shelf in a press.**

**It did not pay the eleven families' problem.** They know. That is all. **It did not answer what the column counts and did not sum it.** **It did not open the form's door and did not refuse it and nobody was asked.** **It did not restore the right of refusal and no character says anybody has one, and the four words at the head of the plank are not reprinted in any of the ten chapters.** **It did not give Sable Venn anything.** **It did not put a word over a heading on a plank, it did not add to the third hand, it did not buy a date, it did not put his name on anything, it did not open the second half of a book with two halves in it, it did not turn the grain year, and it said nothing about a position beneath anything.**

**AND IT SPENT NO PANEL, and a bold scan over the ten chapter files returns ZERO marks.** The register is three for the volume, one is spent at `258:49`–`50`, the second at `276:19`–`20`, and the third belongs to Batch 0005 at the climax. **The standing sentence the volume prints once, at `258:47`, is not printed again, reworded, repeated or paraphrased. ONE non-panel in-world trace is in the batch, at `289:63`, and it is the second and last of the volume.**

### THE FIFTEEN THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM, EVERY ONE OF THEM BOUGHT BY A FAILURE THIS PROJECT HAS ALREADY HAD

1. **The off-by-one between the two columns of `outline/volume-06.md`'s corrected table.** The morning figure in row *N* is reached by the rings figure in row *N*, which means the rings printed in a row are the rings of the day BEFORE that chapter's morning. Chapters 271 to 280 were written on the other alignment. **Chapter 291's morning is sixty-three thousand seven hundred and seventy-eight** if Chapter 290's day is four, and 63,774 is Chapter 290's.
2. **The chapter's-own-day rule, and the eleven wrong-day day-phrases in this batch's own first writing,** every one of them certified legal by the sweep. Ten minutes with a pencil is the instrument.
3. **The span scan at seventy and at forty, with the range, the unit and the METHOD printed, and the range is TWENTY files and not thirty: Chapters 241 to 270 are twenty files on disk, `chapter-0251.md` to `chapter-0270.md`, and there is nothing at 241 to 250.** Zero at seventy, cross-band and within-band, under both methods. **At forty, cross-band against those twenty files is FIVE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FIVE summed over the two hundred chapter pairs and THREE HUNDRED AND SIXTY-SIX as a union of spans, and within-band is ONE THOUSAND ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-SIX.** **The first printing of this item said thirty files and four hundred and fifty-three and one thousand two hundred and ten, and NEITHER NUMBER REPRODUCES UNDER EITHER METHOD, so all three have been re-measured and the two counts are both printed because the method was the unstated half of the error: a record of a scan that does not say whether it sums over pairs or takes a union is a number nobody can check.**
4. **The bold scan must return ZERO marks, because this batch spent no panel,** and seventy-six italic spans and no limit on them.
5. **The chalk count is FIVE for the whole volume so far and all five are in Batch 0001, and this batch added none,** and the third hand has stood in chalk for thirty-nine days.
6. **The string *cannot read* occurs twice and NEITHER is about him,** and a Batch 0004 first writing printed a count of the people who know it in a character's mouth in a press, which is the hardest prohibition in this project and it was broken in one sentence and repaired.
7. **Every money figure is in the outline's table and no new derivation was made, and the one printed working is Chapter 287's,** and **the largest single defect in the first writing was a well-formed number that only the unit-price instrument could see.**
8. **Ask the third question no regex can ask. A clean legality sweep is not a clean chapter,** and this batch is the batch in which the ratio is worst: thirty-one legal phrases and eleven defects in the first writing.
9. **There are TWO non-panel traces in the volume now, at `272:11` and `289:63`,** and Batch 0005 may not add a third, and the standing sentence at `258:47` has not been printed, reworded, repeated or paraphrased anywhere in this batch.
10. **Do not let a later batch restage the day's opening, the day's list, the plank's paint and grain, the bar at the end of the lane, the board's figure at the second light, the woman with a barrow, the count-off-on-her-fingers, the market-day woman with a stick and a board, or the sentence about a man taking a thing off a board.** All were in this batch's first writing and all were reworded on delivery.
11. **The volume's question has now been asked three times and all three shapes are spent: a yard at `260:87`, a doorway at `270:97`–`113`, and a letter at `288`.** A fourth is not available and the close prompt must not ask it in a mouth.
12. **Do not name a weekday, a month-name, an anchor, a stone, the First Witness, or a position beneath anything, and do not use the word *stone* for masonry.** This batch's first writing used it twice for the piece of paving a man of fifty-two weighted a sheet down with.
13. **Do not let a hand-off be wrong about a character's sex or age.** `outline/volume-06.md`'s Chapter 281 and Chapter 283 cards call a woman of fifty-one a man and a man of about thirty-one a woman, and both are wrong and both chapters are right.
14. **Do not prune the state files inside a phase that is also writing prose, and do not edit the archive blocks below the top block of any of the six.**
15. **And the thing no instrument in this project will find: the thing the column counts is still unknown, and nobody may answer it in Batch 0005 either, nobody may sum it, and a writer who needs the number must go and ask a person of a village.**

### WHAT THIS BATCH'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, ALL OF IT CAUGHT, NONE OF IT SURVIVING TO THE DELIVERY

**ELEVEN DAY DEFECTS**, all of them back-references or own-day phrases on the wrong day of the week, all eleven certified legal by the Day-Phrase sweep, all eleven found by putting the ten chapter days beside the thirty-one day-phrases and reading the two lists side by side. **The running count of what the third question has found across Volume 05, Batch 0002, Batch 0003 and this batch is THIRTY-ONE, and the sweep has still never once found one of them.**

**ONE WRONG MONEY FIGURE, and it is the one worth remembering: the levy on nine loads a day at threepence over a hundred and forty days was printed as *three hundred and seventy-eight pounds of a year*, where the figure is three thousand seven hundred and eighty pence, which is fifteen pounds fifteen shillings — a factor of twenty-four, and a number so well formed that every scan in the record returned it as clean.** The unit-price instrument caught it: print the unit price in the sentence, find it, and multiply. The sentence now carries threepence, nine loads, ninepence a day, one shilling and sixpence a day and a hundred and forty days, and no annual total at all.

**ONE HUNDRED AND NINETY-ONE CROSS-BATCH SEVENTY-CHARACTER WINDOWS and SIX HUNDRED AND THIRTY WITHIN-BATCH, from ten sources, and one hundred and eighty-nine of the cross-batch hundred and ninety-one were two sources and both were the hedge sentence of Chapter 254, which this batch quoted in his own interior twice and which Batch 0003's own rule rewords even when the echo is deliberate.** All ten reworded; both scans return zero. **A Batch 0005 writer who wants to quote that sentence again must reword it again.**

**ONE COUNT OF THE PEOPLE WHO KNOW HE CANNOT READ, in a character's mouth, in a press.** The hardest prohibition in this project, broken in a single sentence, and the sentence was replaced with a woman of twenty-nine refusing to let him take a thing off a man who has not offered it — the same beat and none of the prohibition.

**THE WORD *STONE* TWICE, for masonry.** **FOUR META-NARRATION SENTENCES, two of them invisible to the pattern.** **A LEVY PRINTED WRONG, WHICH IS THE SAME CLASS OF ERROR AS EVERY WRONG FIGURE THIS PROJECT HAS HAD AND THE FIRST ONE THAT WAS A FACTOR OF TWENTY-FOUR.** **A `**`-marked span outside a panel: none, and the batch's first writing had none either, because the hand-off forbade it and the writer obeyed it.** **AND ONE THING THE HAND-OFF GOT WRONG AND THE CHAPTER FILES GOT RIGHT, which is a rule rather than a defect and is recorded here because a hand-off is where a volume's rules go quiet: the hand-off forbade the word *anchor* and forbade the word *stone*, and it described *stone* as live when the review of Batch 0003 had already found the premise spent; both prohibitions were kept and the word *stone* was used twice in the first writing anyway, which is what a spent prohibition invites.**
