## VOLUME 06 BATCH 0003 IS DELIVERED — Chapters 271–280, *The Midpoint*. **The live block. The block below it, from `## VOLUME 06 BATCH 0002 IS DELIVERED` down, is archive and is not edited by this phase.**

### WHAT THIS BATCH PAID, ONCE EACH, AND NOT REPEATED

1. **THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT, and it is the series' own sentence and it is fixed: *null zones are revealed as communities that preserved the original right of refusal after the Codification, not random magical failures.*** It landed at Chapter 274 and not at 271, and it landed twice, in two registers, and neither of them is a panel. **A clerk of forty-four delivered the mechanism, in the voice of a man reading a form: the Codification took the right out of the public in a printed line and gave it to nobody, and he has never once in twenty-two years found a document that admitted to having taken something, and a document that does not say what it took was written by people who did not know they were in a room, because there was no room.** **And a woman of fifty-one of Trope delivered the meaning, in an ordinary mouth, unasked: we did not lose it, nobody took it off us, we were never in a room and there was nothing to be in one.**
2. **AND THE RIGHT OF REFUSAL IS NOT RESTORED, and the chapter says that in plain words in an ordinary mouth and not in a panel: we could not refuse the bar, we could not refuse a copy, nobody in this lane has been able to refuse one single thing at any time anybody can remember, and we cannot refuse anything now and we could not before and the two are the same. What we did was stop saying yes, and there was nothing there to say yes to, and the cost was a bell apiece, and it is in that column and there is no name over the column, and I am not going to stand here and tell you that makes it a right. It makes it a bill. Somebody kept the bill. That is all anybody did.** It is a history and not a law, it is in nobody's hands, no Bench has recognised it, and it is not a power and not a remedy.
3. **THE OBJECT, and it is the shape the cards specified and it does not change: a board of a hundred and forty-one names in eleven villages, on the inside of a door in a tower in a village with no bell, found by a clerk of forty-four who was not looking for it, not a charter, not signed, no seal, no witness line and nothing at the foot, the right of refusal written at the head of it in a hand four hundred years old, and the cost of keeping it in a column with no heading over it. IT WAS NOT REMOVED BY ANYBODY, and the chapter says why in a man's own reasoning: a bell is the only thing in eleven villages that anybody has ever gone and looked for, and there has not been one in that village for about two hundred years.**
4. **THE BATCH'S FIRST POSITIVE FINDING ABOUT THE BELL, and the volume's engine turned the right way for the first time: a run of nine consecutive mornings of four, found in the grain, is the only interval this country can prove, and a length is not a day, and a bell can show that a number of days went past and can show nothing else, and the woman of forty-one has known that for twenty-five years and thought it was a defect in her.**
5. **THE PRICE, with the working printed in a yard: two hundred years of a keeper's wage is a thousand and fifty pounds exactly, which is a hundred and fifty-five bells at six pounds fifteen and three pounds fifteen shillings. And a man of sixty-three who has never in eleven years had a village say no to him is standing in the yard and is not asked, and what he says is about his own trade.**
6. **AND THE FINDING UNDERNEATH THE PRICE, which is not in the cards and is the batch's real arithmetic: the whole of the margin of the two villages that have never had a keeper is a carter's year — thirteen thousand eight hundred and sixty pence, which is eleven villages at a keeper's year, less eleven thousand three hundred and forty pence, which is nine of them, is two thousand five hundred and twenty pence, which is ten pounds ten shillings — and a carter has been collecting the other half of it for nineteen years and has never been paid a penny of it and has never once asked what a bar is for.**
7. **THE VOLUME'S CENTRAL PRESSURE, argued a second time in four mouths with a fourth thing in the room that is not a position at all, and the man of thirty-one not asked and in the middle of it, for the third time in thirty days.**
8. **SABLE VENN LOSES SOLE AUTHORITY OVER THE MOVEMENT, which is the series' own sentence, and the sentence that takes it off her is said by a person of a village in a village and not by him, and she agrees with it before she is asked, and she is on no part of the register in any capacity and did not ask to be, and nobody thanked anybody, and she says she is not relieved.**
9. **A REGISTER ANYONE MAY READ AND NOBODY MAY ENTER**, held by the eleven villages and about forty others, kept by a recordwright who is not in it, and bounded by eleven lines of what it does not contain: no gate, no toll, no bar, no day, no date, no bench, no seat, no room with a door on it except a press, no vote, no meeting, no officer, no money, cut out of the back of a bill, with fifty-one places on it and not one village in it.
10. **THE TECHNIQUE, USED A SECOND TIME AND WITH ITS PRICE PAID A SECOND TIME AND THE PRICE BEING A DIFFERENT PRICE**: the first time it was that two women learned each other's names; the second time it is that the two men at the end of it have nobody to put the round to, and a third thing on a door that neither of them will ever see.
11. **THE NETWORK, with the evidence for it and the evidence is arithmetic and not opinion: the same round of eleven villages in three instruments that have got nothing to do with one another, and no man's cart would choose it, and the only record in the March that holds all eleven is a boy's book that is five years old.**
12. **THE BATCH'S CLIMAX, and it is a failure of an instrument and not a discovery of a power: a boy of seventeen tries to read a board of a hundred and forty-one names with a line book and cannot, and the finding is about the book, and he writes nothing, and he says he will think about it and will not be hurried, and he goes and does his day.**
13. **THE PANEL, which is the second of the volume's three and the only one in this batch, in two plain lines, about a hundred and forty-one names and a column with no heading, deciding nothing, and whose undecided question is the batch's only unanswered question that anybody wrote down on purpose.**
14. **AND THE ROUND NUMBER NOBODY CHOSE: the board reads sixty-three thousand five hundred and ten on the first morning of Chapter 251 and reads sixty-three thousand seven hundred and ten on the first morning of Chapter 281, and the difference is exactly two hundred, and no instrument in this county can say what the two hundred was for, and there is no column for it.**

### WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT PAY, AND WHY NOT

1. **It did not ask the volume's question a third time.** It has now been asked twice, at `260:87` in a yard to nobody without a mark and at `270:97`–`113` in a doorway to a person with one, and in Chapter 279 it is referred to once as a mark at the end of a question in a book and put in nobody's mouth. **A third asking is a restage of a spent device and this batch refused it at the cost of the one thing it most wanted to do, which was to have a person say the sentence out loud and be wrong about it.** Nobody answers it. The System does not raise it. It is not compressed into one word.
2. **It did not tell the eleven families anything.** They are named in Chapter 280 as ninety acres that eleven families are grazing and paying nothing for and have never been told about, and nobody in eleven villages has been told anything, and the reason is still the reason Batch 0002 printed: the batch's problem is that nobody in eleven villages can produce a day, and a fact told to eleven families without a day on it is eleven families in a lane with nothing to be right about. **A new reason arrived in this batch and it is worse and it is honest: a name said out loud in a yard is a person in a room, and the woman who wanted it said in a yard that she would rather be a coward about it for the rest of her life.**
3. **It did not answer what the column counts.** Nobody added it up, a man of fifty-two refused to on purpose and out loud, the panel left the question on the page, and the whole of Chapter 280's last movement is a man who could guess in about four seconds choosing not to guess because he cannot produce a record.
4. **It did not go into a tower with a door on the way in, it did not open the form's fourth line, it did not refuse the fourth line either, and nobody was asked about either.** **It did not undo anything, did not relieve anybody, did not redeem anybody, did not forgive anybody and did not thank anybody.** The third hand is still in the chalk and has not been added to in twenty-nine days. The figure at the foot of the copy is still in the county and still unspoken about. The two dates are bought and the two names are on one thing. The carrying is off nothing. The leg is no better and no count of days is printed. The second half of the book with two halves in it was not opened. The boundary is still in the twenty-second year of the board and unprovable and the eleven families still know nothing. **The chapter that carries the batch's climax does not have the man of thirty-one offer to take a figure off a piece of paper, does not have him apologise, and does not have him say he is sorry for it.**

### THE NINE THINGS THIS BATCH'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, ALL NINE CAUGHT BY THE THIRD QUESTION, WHICH IS THE ONLY INSTRUMENT THAT FOUND ANYTHING

**Every one of them is a back-reference put on the wrong day of the week, and the Day-Phrase sweep returned every one of them legal, and that is now the eleventh class of defect this project has produced and the first time one batch has produced nine of them at once.** `273:111` put a man with a barrow four days ago where he was seven; `272:87` put a line said on the fifth day of the fifty-ninth week on the second; `275:13` and `275:31` put two references to Chapter 253 in the fifty-ninth week when Chapter 253 is the third day of the **fifty-eighth**; `276:24` and `276:38` put a tower reading three days before when it was two and a kitchen nine days ago when it was eight; `277:13` put a sum done in six days when it was twelve, and `277:31` and `277:43` put two references to Chapter 265 nine days ago when they were twelve; `279:11` put the technique nine days ago when it was twelve. **The cheapest instrument is ten chapter days against every day-phrase in the batch, side by side, and it takes ten minutes, and on this batch it was the only instrument in the project that found anything at all.**

### AND WHAT THE REVIEW OF BATCH 0003 FOUND IN THE DELIVERY AND IN THE RECORD OF IT, EIGHT FINDINGS, ALL EIGHT CLOSED, AND THE FULL RECEIPT IS `state/current.md` §12

**NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO CARD WAS CHANGED, THE PLANNED PLOT IS UNCHANGED, AND ALL FOUR PROSE REPAIRS WERE MADE INSIDE EXISTING LINES, so every `chapter-02xx.md:n` citation in this file and in the other five state files still points where it pointed on delivery.** Three were in the prose and one was a record that had been describing the prose wrongly for a day, and what this batch's own instruments had certified clean, a review found by reading and by re-running the instruments against the files instead of against the block.

1. **`276:58` — A WRONG DAY OF THE WEEK IN A BAR, AND THE ELEVENTH CLASS, AND THE THIRD TIME THE THIRD QUESTION WAS THE ONLY THING THAT COULD HAVE FOUND IT.** A woman of about fifty at the bar, twenty-two years at it, said there had not been nine there since a timber cart and a man with lime came through on the fourth day of the fifty-ninth week. **It is the fourth day of the sixtieth: `268:3`, thirteen on the board and nine strangers out of the thirteen, and the ninth is correct and was left alone. The fourth day of the fifty-ninth week is `261:3`, where the board went up five and the cart LEFT.** Repaired, and her list made to match `268:3` — two men with a barrow, not two barrows. **The Day-Phrase sweep returned the phrase legal, because the phrase IS legal; a sweep cannot know what a bar remembers.**
2. **`275:83` — AN UNPAYABLE FORWARD REFERENCE AND A MISATTRIBUTION IN ONE SENTENCE.** It promised a press eleven miles off on the sixth day of the sixty-first week, which is Chapter 277, and Chapter 277 is a yard at Coldharrow; and it gave the argument to a woman with four hundred pieces of paper in nine years, which is Sable Venn's figure and she was not in that stair. **The woman is Isel Cardew, fifty-one, at `274:87`: *it is in that column, and there is no name over the column*.** The chain — a heading over a column is a category, a category is a room — is kept whole and the day went, because a forward reference a batch does not pay is a debt and not a hook.
3. **`271:117` AND `274:47` — FORTY SHARED SEVENTY-CHARACTER RUNS AGAINST CHAPTER 270 THAT THIS BATCH'S OWN ZERO HIDDEN.** Both reworded, both recorded with the before and the after at `state/current.md` §2 item 4. **The second is a deliberate and signalled callback and it was reworded anyway, because this project's rule is that a shared run is a defect and is reworded rather than left, and a rule a writer can set aside on the grounds that the echo was on purpose is not a rule.**
4. **AND FOUR FIGURES IN THE RECORD, none of them in a chapter.** The Batch 0004 hand-off gave three accounts of its own arithmetic trap and blamed the wrong artifact, so a writer could not have known which of the file's tables to trust; the span range was called twenty-nine files and is thirty; the `stone` prohibition was carried into Batch 0004 in the present tense on a premise Batch 0003 had already spent; and the refrain union was reported as three and is five. **Every instrument was then re-run over the ten chapter files and every figure in this block is the re-measured one, and the standing instruction of this project is unchanged: where a state figure and the chapters disagree, the chapters win.**

### AND SIX MORE THE PROJECT'S OWN INSTRUMENTS FOUND, WHICH IS THE CLASS A LATER BATCH WILL MAKE AGAIN

- **TWELVE WEEKDAYS, in six chapters, in the plainest possible English, against a standing prohibition.** The plural-safe pattern with a plural on every alternative including Monday returned twelve and it is now zero.
- **THE RECORDWRIGHT'S NINE WORDS WERE SAID A FOURTH TIME, at `274:7`, inside a report of a clerk's yard — and that would have made `270:85` false, which is the exact defect the repair of 27 September 2026 made, in the same project, eight weeks apart, about the same nine words.** It is the third time this project has put them in the wrong mouth. **A Batch 0004 writer must treat the sentence as spent, and the way to treat a spent sentence is not to reword it.**
- **THE WORD *STONE* SIX TIMES, for masonry**, in a project where the prohibition is about a relic and a scanner cannot tell a stair from a relic.
- **A LIST OF TWENTY RING-COUNTS INVENTED IN CHAPTER 271 AND WRONG IN ITS TENTH ITEM AND IN ITS LENGTH.** The board chain has no formula and the writer of Chapter 271 wrote one anyway, out of twenty figures, and the figure at position ten should have been a five and was a four. **It is out.** What replaced it is a run of nine mornings of four found in the grain, which is better, is datable by nobody, and cannot be got wrong.
- **THREE HUNDRED AND SEVEN SHARED SEVENTY-CHARACTER WINDOWS on the first writing, from thirteen sources, and every one has been reworded.** The sources are named at `state/current.md` §11 because they are the ones a later batch will make again, and the list begins with a reprinted line of a printed form and a bell-founder's uncomputed cause and a plank's paint and grain and a day's opening in the shape of the sixth day of the sixtieth week.
- **FOUR META-NARRATION SENTENCES, one of which the pattern did not catch: *it is not the volume's cost*, in Chapter 274, which is this project's own vocabulary in a chapter's mouth.** The sweep is a shape and not a reading, and that sentence is the proof.

### AND TWO DEFECTS THIS PHASE FOUND IN THE RECORD OF THE BATCH BELOW, WHICH IS ARCHIVE AND WAS NOT EDITED, AND WHICH ARE RECORDED HERE BECAUSE THEY ARE THE SAME CLASS AS THE NINE

1. **`state/continuity.md`'s live Batch 0002 block said the Coldharrow board reads *sixty-three thousand five hundred and forty-two* in two places.** The figure is sixty-three thousand six hundred and forty-two. The repair of 27 September 2026 corrected `270:29` and recorded that it had, and did not correct the block that describes the chapter. **That is the eleventh time this project has fixed a figure in the prose and left it wrong in the record of the prose, and it is the reason a state file is a fact about a range and not a fact about the chapters.** The correction is in the live Batch 0003 block and the archive was not edited.
2. **The same block still said that *about thirty-one of the about hundred and forty people in the village are not on* the Ellerby board.** The repair of 27 September 2026 removed that sentence from `266:31`, from `266:97` and from two state files, and it survived in this one. **A sentence that does not add up against a village of about a hundred and forty is a sentence a reader would have taken for canon, and it survived a repair that had already identified it as a defect elsewhere, which means the repair was measured against a list of sites rather than against the files.**

### AND ONE META-NARRATION SENTENCE STILL IN THE MANUSCRIPT, FOUND BY READING CHAPTER 255 AND NOT BY ANY INSTRUMENT, RECORDED RATHER THAN REPAIRED

**`255:43` puts the phrase *the fourth volume of your own life* into a recordwright's mouth in a garden in the Briar March.** It is this project's own structure in a chapter's dialogue, it is under fifty characters so the seventy-character span scan cannot see it, it is not one of the four named meta strings so the meta sweep cannot see it, and **the meta sweep as specified would not catch it, which is the finding.** A sentence that refers to the shape of the thing it is inside is the hardest of this project's defects to instrument for and the easiest for a reader to feel. **It is not repaired here because this phase is forbidden to restart a completed chapter and because Chapter 255 is nine batches behind this one; it is recorded so that a repair pass finds it by reading and not by scanning, and a Batch 0004 writer must not repeat the class.**

### AND THE CHAPTER LENGTHS, WHICH ARE A MEASUREMENT AND NOT A CLAIM

**Ten chapters, 143,855 bytes in all, and the mean is 14,386, which is above the nine-to-thirteen-thousand guide in `outline/volume-06.md` and is not a padded batch.** By chapter, measured on disk after the review of Batch 0003 and its four in-line repairs: 15,926 / 12,943 / 17,373 / 19,257 / 13,963 / 10,474 / 14,750 / 13,418 / 12,797 / 12,954. **The three that ran long are the two chapters that carry the volume's turn — 273 finds the board and 274 lands the reversal — and a fourth is the chapter that spends the price, and cutting any paragraph out of any of them removes a finding, and a batch that gets under the guide by cutting a finding has met the guide by failing the volume.** The two that came in under it, 276 and 280, are the panel and the climax, and both are shorter than the batch's own middle for the same reason: a panel decides nothing and a boy's failure to read a wall is one sentence that cannot be stretched. **A Batch 0004 writer may take the guide as a guide and should not take this as a licence, and `outline/volume-06.md` now prints this range under the Batch 0004 guide so that nobody pads or cuts a scene to hit a number.**

### THE FIFTEEN THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM

1. **The board chain.** 63,647 to 63,706 in nine exact steps, 63,710 on the first morning of Batch 0004. There is no formula and a later pass must not go looking for one.
2. **The chapter's-own-day rule, and the nine wrong-day back-references a legality sweep certified.** A clean sweep is not a clean chapter and this batch proves it harder than any other.
3. **The span scan at seventy and at forty, with the unit and the threshold and the range declared.** **Zero at seventy and nine hundred and twenty-six at forty, and the range is THIRTY files on disk and not the twenty-nine this block, `state/continuity.md` and the Batch 0004 hand-off all called it.** **The review of Batch 0003 found this block's own zero was hiding forty shared seventy-character runs against that range, from two sources, and both are reworded and the figure is measured now and not asserted.**
4. **The bold scan must return exactly four marks, which is one panel in two lines at `276:19`–`20`, and nothing else.** Seventy-six italic spans and no limit on them.
5. **The chalk count is FIVE for the whole volume so far, all five in Batch 0001, and this batch added none.**
6. **The string *cannot read* occurs three times and only one of them is about him.** Do not print a count of anybody who knows anything about him, in any file, for any reason, and do not let Chapter 280's paragraph be read as a disclosure: it is the absence of the asking that is the finding, and a Batch 0004 writer must not convert it into one.
7. **Every figure in these ten chapters is in the money table except one derivation, the two hundred years, and the working is printed in the fiction at `275:13`.** And the one figure that is neither in the table nor new is the ten pounds ten shillings at `275:77`, and it is the difference between two table rows and the working is printed.
8. **Ask the third question no regex can ask.** Thirty-three legal phrases and nine defects in the first writing, **and a tenth in the delivery at `276:58`, on a day of the week, in a bar, that every sweep above had certified.**
9. **The four weekdays and the eleven spans are gone, the standing sentence at `258:47` has not been printed, reworded, repeated or paraphrased, and the ONE non-panel trace in the batch is at `272:11` and may not be exceeded by more than one more in this volume.**
10. **Do not let a later batch restage the day's opening, the day's list, a plank's paint and grain, the bar at the end of the lane, the board's figure at the second light, or the sentence *you have just said nine words about a week of four*.**
11. **Do not let the two boards rhyme.** Ellerby's is public and outside and complete and a floor. The board in the tower is private and inside a door and a list of people and a hand that a clerk can date. Chapter 273 makes the distinction in the fiction.
12. **Do not name a weekday, a month-name, an anchor, a stone, the First Witness, or a position beneath anything.**
13. **Do not let a hand-off be wrong about a character's sex or age. THE CLERK OF FORTY-FOUR IS A MAN AND IS NOT NAMED, and no chapter of Volume 06 named him and a Batch 0004 writer may not.** Isel Cardew is a woman of fifty-one. Petrow Sill is a man of thirty-five. Sable Venn is a woman.
14. **Do not prune the state files inside a phase that is also writing prose, and do not edit the archive blocks below the top block of any of the six.**
15. **AND THE FIFTEENTH, WHICH NO INSTRUMENT IN THIS PROJECT WILL FIND: the column of a hundred and forty-one figures has not been added up and is not going to be by anybody, and that is a decision a man of fifty-two made in a stair in front of twenty people, and it is the batch's only unanswered question that anybody wrote down on purpose.** Nobody may answer it in Batch 0004 or Batch 0005, nobody may sum it, and a writer who needs the number must go and ask a person of a village.

## VOLUME 06 BATCH 0002 IS DELIVERED — Chapters 261–270, *The Price Of A Date*. **The live block. The block below it, from `## VOLUME 06 BATCH 0001 IS DELIVERED` down, is archive and is not edited by this phase.** Chapters 1–270 are canon; `outline/series.md` outranks `outline/volume-06.md` and this file.

### WHAT THIS BATCH PAID, ONCE EACH, AND NOT REPEATED

1. **The consequence of a piece of paper having sat under a cart seat for a day: it leaves the county unread and nobody has anything to unpick, and the only thing that happens instead is that a day is finished when somebody decides it and cannot be took back.**
2. **The brass, spent once: three people, three reasons, one pull, one ring, and a county that can prove three and not one.**
3. **THE TWENTY-FIVE-YEAR ARITHMETIC, in a woman of forty-one's mouth: 54,750 up in twenty-five years, 36,500 of it the four bells, 18,250 everything else, so about two rings a day sit on top of the clock and about two rings a day does not touch most days, and the days it does not touch are the days the board says four on — so four is the ordinary number in this county and not a remarkable one, and a figure that cannot be told from another figure is not a record.**
4. **THE SENTENCE THE BATCH TURNS ON, in her mouth: a number that anybody can move is a number that has to be asked about, and there is exactly one person in this county you can be asked, and for twenty-five years that was a comfort and it is a door.**
5. **A man who has been standing in a yard doing nothing for ten mornings can account for a bell he did not account for before, and it is the first use of anything he has learned in this country, and it is small.**
6. **THE PRICE OF A DATE, twice: £2 7s exactly, thirty-one days of the man who walks the road and sixpence, and a year of the woman who keeps the count less £2 18s — and the finding that the figure was never a price at all and was the entire annual wage of a woman spent in an afternoon by three men.**
7. **THE CHAMBER'S ANSWER, four hundred miles off: correct about everything and wrong about the place in three ways, and one of the three is that it would file this county's only name in a district that has never heard of it.**
8. **THE CENTRAL PRESSURE, said out loud in full by three ordinary people in ordinary mouths, and the finding that they are three prices and not three positions, and that the argument has been going for nine years by three people who were not waiting for him.**
9. **A SECOND VILLAGE WITH A DIFFERENT ARITHMETIC: a board of two hundred and nine names and no figures, where every name is a person somebody can go and ask, and the same four lines are worth nothing there and are worth something again the moment a person in that village stands in a doorway.**
10. **THE TECHNIQUE, DEFINED ONCE IN THE FICTION, WITH ITS PRICE: you may put two things next to each other, you may not make them one, and the price is that the two people in the middle of them learn each other's names, and there is no instrument that stops it.**
11. **Sable Venn and Ilyan working together for the first time, badly, and the badness being that the method works, and SHE wrote the batch's only document in her own hand and could not read it back, and the consequence of her own nine words said in her own mouth and not apologised for, and the thing underneath it: it worked four hundred times because of what she would not know and not because of what she could not do.** **THE DELIVERY GAVE THE DOCUMENT TO A WOMAN WHO DID NOT EXIST AND SPLIT SABLE VENN'S FOUR POINTS ACROSS THREE SPEAKERS, AND ITS OWN STATE FILES AGREED WITH EACH OTHER ABOUT IT; SEE THE REPAIR IN `state/current.md`.**
12. **THE FIRST ROUTE OUT OF A COUNTY THAT ANYBODY CHOSE, and it is two hundred words in Sable Venn's hand with no name and no day at the foot of it, put on a barrow at a gate that is not shut, and the bottom two inches of it were empty when it went and were not empty when it came back.**
13. **THE BATCH'S REAL COST: a figure of his own, from the first morning, in a stranger's hand at the foot of a copy of four lines he did not read, given because a woman of twenty-nine asked him for a number and he had nothing else to give; and a woman of forty-one working out in a yard that a number on a wall is a thing about a village and a number on a piece of paper is a thing about a person, and not saying it is his, and not being told she is sorry for it.**
14. **A hundred and thirty-two, printed on a cart's side, which is the whole of nineteen days' rings, whose last day was a day of four, which is the one day out of the nineteen you could not find with a lamp and a boy — and 63,510 + 132 = 63,642, which is the figure at the head of the board on the morning the copy comes back and is the number the batch turns on.**
15. **AND THE VOLUME'S QUESTION, ASKED A SECOND TIME AND NOT ANSWED EITHER TIME. THE HEADING ON DELIVERY SAID *ANSWERED TWICE BY FOUR WORDS* AND THE PARAGRAPH UNDER IT SAID THE OPPOSITE, IN THE SAME FILE, TWENTY LINES APART; THE HEADING WAS WRONG AND THE PARAGRAPH WAS RIGHT, AND *four words* COULD NOT HAVE ANSWERED IT IN ANY CASE.** A carter of thirty-eight, in a yard, at the last bell, to nobody, without a mark, twenty-five words, at `260:87`. A boy of seventeen, in a doorway, to a person, longer, **with a mark on the end of it**, written in a book that has never held a question, at `270:97`–`113`, and the answer was *I do not know* and the boy said *that is two of us*. **Nobody answered either. The System said nothing, and was not asked, and the question is not compressed into one word anywhere.**

### WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT PAY, AND DID NOT TRY TO

**It did not deliver the volume's midpoint reversal**, which belongs at Chapter 271, 272, 273 or 274 in the **second half** of Batch 0003: *null zones are revealed as communities that preserved the original right of refusal after the Codification, not random magical failures.* **Chapters 261 to 270 treat a null zone as damage — a road that ends and a lane that does not, a name that drops out of a record and comes back, a person recognised in one doorway and not in the next — and the reversal is that eleven villages declined to be in the network rather than lost it. No chapter here may be read as paying it, and no character in these ten chapters speculates it and no character in these ten chapters is right about it. Ellerby's board of names is an arithmetic difference and not a preserved right, and the custom of ringing by whoever is nearest is about a bell.**

**It spent no panel.** The register is three for the volume, one is spent at `258:49`–`50`, and the second belongs to Batch 0003 and the third to Batch 0005. **The hand-off prompt for this band said a writer might write one and that was wrong, and the correction is recorded in the cards, in the outline and here.**

**It did not resolve the split with Sera Quill.** She said the nine words a third time at `270:85` and it was not softened, and the engine is not a quarrel and is not resolved by being agreed. **AND AFTER THE REPAIR OF 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 THAT IS ALSO TRUE OF THE COUNT: she said the nine words three times in this batch, at `261`, at `262` and at `270:85`, and the delivery had her saying them four times, the fourth inside a speech that was not hers, which would have made `270:85` false.**

**It did not tell the eleven families anything**, and the reason is now printed in the fiction at `265:53` and is the volume's and not a convenience.

**It did not put the four lines into a room.** Nobody from any building with a yard came to Coldharrow. A thing came and it was a document, and a document in this country is a date nobody can produce. **AND THE FOURTH LINE OF THE FORM, WHICH IS A DOOR, IS STILL A DOOR: nobody has opened it and nobody has refused it and nobody was asked, and it belongs to Chapter 271.**

**It did not undo anything and it did not relieve anybody.** Nobody is forgiven, nobody is redeemed, nobody is relieved, the eleven families still know nothing, the form's door is unopened, the third hand is still in the chalk, the sheet is still on a barrow with no name and no day at the foot of it, the sawyer still has not read what he is carrying, and the batch's last line is a boy of seventeen going to do his day because somebody in that village had to count the bells.

**It did not name a weekday, a month, an anchor, a stone, the First Witness, a position beneath anything, or a person who is not already in the fiction. **THE REVIEW OF 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 FOUND THAT ONE CHAPTER HAD SAID *THE FOUR DAYS OF THIS MONTH*, WHICH IS A MONTH, AND IT IS OUT; A WEEKDAY SCAN, A MONTH-NAMED SCAN AND THE OTHER EIGHT SWEEPS NOW RETURN CLEAN OVER THE REPAIRED BATCH.** It did not give the man of thirty-one an office, a party, a bench, a body, a stage, a promotion, or a name on anything in the March. It did not print a count of the people who know he cannot read, in this file or in any other, and it did not tell anybody in the fiction.**

### WHAT THIS BATCH'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, ALL OF IT CAUGHT BY THE PROJECT'S OWN INSTRUMENTS

**SIX THINGS, and the re-scan was run after the fixes and not before them, and the number is six and not five and the sixth was found while measuring the first five.**

1. **TWELVE EMPHASIS-BOLD SPANS, in Chapters 265, 266 and 269, on a batch whose cards say no panel and whose volume's register is three.** Bold is for panel lines and nothing else, and a bold instrument that counts two things is an instrument that does not work. All twelve are converted to italic and the bold scan now returns zero marks anywhere in the ten files.
2. **TWO HUNDRED AND THIRTY-ONE SEVENTY-CHARACTER RUNS SHARED WITH CHAPTERS 201 TO 260.** The duplicate scan is a span scan and the unit is the span, at a threshold of seventy characters, and a run shared with the previous batch is a defect and is reworded rather than left. The re-scan returns **zero**. The thirteen sources are the day's-rise opening, the market-day woman with a stick and a board, the count-off-on-her-fingers, *because he has not written down what is said in a yard since a market town four hundred miles off*, *about nine hundred people who cannot pay a pound three shillings and sixpence for a day out of a village*, *whatever she decided about him happened in that second*, the hedge sentence of the fourth day of the fifty-eighth week, his own sentence of the same day about the first one when there are two, *for about as long as it takes to cross a yard*, the description of the woman with the barrow, the sentence about a man taking a thing off a board, and **the nine words of the recordwright, which are a refrain and are the one thing in the list that cannot be reworded without changing the volume, and were therefore broken at the attribution twice and never at the sentence.**
3. **THE THIRD QUESTION FOUND FIVE DATE DEFECTS, and this is the eleventh time it has caught anything and the second time it has beaten the legality sweep, which returned zero illegal over all seventeen day-phrases.** Two chapter openings carried the previous batch's opening verbatim; a back-reference in Chapter 269 put a sentence on the wrong day of that week; and two day-count figures were wrong — a woman of nine years' standing said she had been living on her nine words for eighteen days when Chapter 259 is the ninth day and Chapter 268 is the eighteenth, so it is nine, and in Chapter 269 the same two figures are ten and nineteen and not eighteen. **A legality sweep that returns zero is not evidence that a chapter's dates are right.**
4. **A MONEY FIGURE THAT WAS NOT IN THE TABLE AT ALL, caught while re-reading rather than by the figure script: a woman of forty with a barrow was given a wage of three and fourpence a day, and there is no such figure in `outline/volume-06.md` and no working for it, and it was struck.** This is the eleventh time this project has put a figure in a chapter that is not in the table, and it is the reason the instruction is *print the unit price in the sentence, find it, and multiply* and the reason a figure the script cannot find is a figure nobody checked.
5. **THREE META-NARRATION SENTENCES, in Chapters 261, 266 and 269, in the shape *this batch*, *this chapter* and *the finding of this chapter*.** Batch 0001 uses *this volume* three times and *this chapter* never. All three are reworded and the meta sweep now returns zero.
6. **AND ONE THING THE SCANS DID NOT CATCH AND A PERSON DID, which is recorded because it is the class of error the project keeps making in a new costume: the first writing of Chapter 262 opened with the previous batch's opening verbatim and the first writing of Chapter 266 opened with Chapter 260's opening verbatim, and BOTH of those were the same chapter the rewordings fixed, and neither the weekday scan nor the month scan nor the panel scan nor the money script would ever have seen either of them.** The span scan saw them. **The lesson is the fourth time this project has learned it in this form: the instrument that catches a copy is the one that looks for copies, and the instruments that look for other things are not going to find it for you.**

### THE FIFTEEN THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM

The fifteen are `state/current.md` §9 of this batch's block, item by item, and they are not repeated here. **What is repeated here is the one that has cost the most: the board chain, the chapter's-own-day rule, the span scan at seventy and at forty with its unit and its range declared, the bold scan returning exactly zero, the chalk count staying at five, the *cannot read* string belonging to Sable Venn, and the fact that a count in these files that carries no line number and no range beside it is a claim and not a measurement.**

## VOLUME 06 BATCH 0001 IS DELIVERED — Chapters 251–260, *The Bell, And A Count With No Date In It*. **The live block. The block below it, from `## VOLUME 05 IS CLOSED` down, is archive and is not edited by this phase.** Chapters 1–260 are canon; Volume 05 is closed; `outline/series.md` outranks `outline/volume-06.md` and this file.

### WHAT THIS BATCH PAID, ONCE EACH, AND NOT REPEATED

**It planned Volume 06 and wrote its first ten chapters.** `outline/volume-06.md` did not exist and the Volume 05 close was forbidden to write one, so this phase wrote it, and it is the volume's spec, its calendar, its money with the working beside every figure, its fifteen prohibitions, its ten devices, and detailed cards for all ten of these chapters. **And it wrote the ten chapters, and the plan held without a repair afterwards, which is the first time in this project that has been true of a volume-opening batch and the first time in five batches of Volume 05 and the only time in six batches that the plan and the prose have not had to be reconciled afterwards.** That is worth recording plainly and it is worth not making a rule of, because the plan was written by the same hand in the same hour.

### WHAT THE BATCH PAID THE VOLUME

1. **A new region with its own instrument, and the instrument is one notch bigger than Volume 05's rather than a repeat of it.** Volume 05's problem was two calendars naming one day. **Volume 06's is a counter with no units in it: a village that can prove how many times and can prove before or after and cannot prove when, and the engine of the whole volume is that a day's figure is a weighted number and a weighted number cannot be turned back into what was done.**
2. **The volume's central arithmetic, exact to the penny and printed in a cart yard: the excess of the whole of the gate levy over the whole of the walking is £5 5s, and the bell-keeper's wage is £5 5s, and eleven villages' whole margin is £57 15s, which is the entire margin of a lane eleven villages own.** A village that wants to be left alone has one lever and it is the wages of one woman, and nobody in eleven villages has ever noticed.
3. **Four of the series' own devices, delivered once each:** a counter with no units; a hand in chalk at the foot of somebody else's instrument; a refusal by a boy of seventeen against the man helping, and the boy is right; and a copy of a document arriving in a place it was not measured in with nobody having read it.
4. **The volume's split, open and unrepaired: *a count that leaves the board is a count somebody can use*, said twice, and a man who has said out loud that he is going to pay for a date and have somebody else pay it too.**
5. **The engine, in the middle of the batch and in nobody's mouth: a schedule is not a river, a bell is a schedule, the first bell of a Coldharrow day is a height above nothing, and a copy of an order that names a height without naming a sill is load-bearing in a country of two hundred and ten people and not a curiosity.** The four lines came into the March at a cost of threepence and five shillings and four days and nobody in two hundred and ten people did anything about it, **and that is not inertia and it is not a decision anybody took, and a bench that is sent nothing writes down that it was sent nothing, and a place with no party is a place that can be done to.**
6. **The volume's new question, asked once, in a yard, by a carter of thirty-eight, in twenty-five words, at the last bell, to nobody, and not answered: it does not run north to south, it runs from village to village, and I have never found out who laid it out that way.**
7. **A cast of five March people who each want something ordinary, none an antagonist, none a production, none relieved, and the incompleteness in each of them named by an ordinary person and not by the man.**
8. **Sable Venn, a woman, in her own country, who said nine words before she was asked and who has spent nine years saying she has never read a record in her life and who has just questioned that herself, standing in a yard, to a man of thirty-one.** Not thanked, not on a paper, did not ask to be, did not win, did not lose.

### THE TWO THINGS THIS BATCH DID NOT PAY, AND NEITHER IS TO BE PAID BY A LATER PASS BY ACCIDENT

**THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT REVERSAL, which belongs at Chapter 271, 272, 273 or 274 in the second half of Batch 0003: *null zones are revealed as communities that preserved the original right of refusal after the Codification, not random magical failures.*** Chapters 251 to 260 treat a null zone as damage, and the reversal is that the eleven villages declined to be in the network rather than lost it. **No pass may move it earlier and no pass may read this batch as paying it.**

**THE SPLIT WITH SERA QUILL, and the right of refusal, and the right of refusal as a right anybody has, and the volume's new question in its stated form.** None of them is resolved and none of them is summarised by anybody.

### THE FIFTEEN THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM WAS BOUGHT BY A FAILURE THIS PROJECT HAD BEFORE THIS BATCH

1. **Going looking for a formula.** There is none in Volume 06 and there is not going to be one, and the reason there is none is the volume's central finding. The check is the board chain.
2. **Reading the board's opening figure out of a block** instead of computing 6 × 1,460 + 25 × 2,190 = 63,510, **and the twenty-four-and-seven that this file printed on its first writing was not a derivation of anything and is the reason this item now names the six and the twenty-five and the two lines of the fiction that carry them.**
3. **Believing the chalk count is what the outline's table says.** The plan listed five chalk sites and named 260; the measurement gives five and names 257, and the outline has been corrected to the measurement.
4. **Treating a clean day-phrase sweep as a clean chapter.** Fourteen phrases, zero illegal, and the third question found four date defects in this batch's own first writing.
5. **The weekday scan.** It returned five on the first writing, including the sentence *it was on a Tuesday except that she does not know what a Tuesday is*, and it caught all five.
6. **Bold meaning more than one thing.** The first writing carried thirty-nine emphasis-bold spans. Bold is panel lines only, or the bold instrument stops being worth running.
7. **A seventy-character carry-over is a defect and a forty-character one is not.** One was found, out of `245`, and reworded rather than left.
8. **The hundred and forty days.** The route-man says out loud that it is wrong by about seven in the hundred in a year about every seven years, and a later pass may not quietly correct it and may not quietly use 110.
9. **A bell is £1 10s more than a year of the woman and not £3**, and the outline's own money table was wrong on its first writing and has been amended to the working.
10. **Nine loads a day and nine carts a day are different quantities in different volumes.** The nine is forced by the arithmetic: 3n − 18 = 9, so n = 9.
11. **A count of the people who know he cannot read, in any file, for any reason.** Chapter 260 handles the thread as a device and not as a disclosure.
12. **A hand-off being wrong about a character's sex or age.** Sable Venn is a woman and the chapter files say so and the bible does not; Tarin Keel is fifty-two in the chapters and forty-six in the bible. The chapter file wins both times and this is the second time.
13. **The word *anchor*, a stone, the position beneath the basin, or the First Witness.** The scans return zero for all of them over the ten chapters and a Batch 0002 writer has to keep it that way.
14. **A person of the Briar March being turned into a production.** The self-arrived class is restated for this volume and is empty, and it is empty for a structural reason and not by luck.
15. **A batch that prunes the state files while it is also writing prose.** The recommendation stands and it is still not a thing a writing batch should do.

### WHAT THIS BATCH'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, ALL OF IT CAUGHT BY THE PROJECT'S OWN INSTRUMENTS, ALL OF IT CORRECTED IN THE PROSE BEFORE DELIVERY, AND RECORDED HERE BECAUSE A LATER PASS NEEDS TO KNOW THE SCANS RUN AND ARE NOT CLEAN ON THE FIRST PASS

**Nine things, and none of them is a plot defect.** Five weekday names at `254:3`, `256:7`, `256:83` and twice at `260`. **Thirty-nine emphasis-bold spans across nine chapters, and the bold instrument returned forty-one lines where Volume 05's returns six.** One mixed bold-and-quote mark at `260:71`. **Four board-figure date errors: a figure quoted off the board in Chapter 252 was the previous morning's; the figure quoted in a recordwright's mouth in Chapter 255 was two days stale; Chapter 257 said the night had added seven when the figure it gave required five; and Chapter 258 gave the first day's figure to the second day while Chapter 259 called thirteen rings nine.** Two board-count sites in the outline's table that the prose does not match, corrected in the outline to the measurement. And **one money figure wrong in the outline's own money table before a single chapter of Volume 06 was written, which is the whole reason that table prints a working beside every figure.** **Eight of the nine were caught by instruments this project built after being bitten. The ninth was caught before there was any prose at all, by a subtraction.**

### THE RECEIPT OF THE REVIEW OF THIS PHASE, AND THE NINE THINGS THE BATCH'S SECOND WRITING GOT WRONG, WHICH THE REVIEW FOUND AND WHICH ARE ALL FIXED

**The review of `next-0002` ran on the ten chapters, the outline, the six state files and the hand-off prompt; its log is `logs/next-0002.review.log`, and the full receipt with the repair in each item is §11 of `state/current.md`.** **It found the volume's central number not derivable from the fiction, the volume's own device contradicted three times by the prose, one direct contradiction between `259:73` and `260:85`, one sentence of garbled arithmetic, seven sites of day-drift, one garbled unit of distance, a hand-off prompt pointing at ten chapter cards that were not on disk, and four state measurements that did not reproduce — and it confirmed clean: the weekday scan, the bold scan, the day-phrase sweep, the ten-step board chain, the whole money table, and no controller file touched. The prose was reworded inside lines. No chapter was restarted, no chapter lost a line, no line number in any state file moved, and the planned plot did not move at all.**

**The nine things the batch's second writing got wrong, and the specific irony the review named is that the two instruments this batch's own record claimed had run and passed — the chalk count and the third question on a date — are the two the prose broke:**

1. **The board's opening figure was 50,370 on a twenty-four-and-seven split, and the prose says six and twenty-five in four places.** **Now 63,510, on 6 × 1,460 + 25 × 2,190, and the whole chain moved by 13,140.** One clause went into `253:39` so the fiction carries the derivation. **A state file that prints a working beside a figure still does not check it, and this project has now learned that four times.**
2. **`256:3` called the sixth morning the seventh.**
3. **`257:3` called the seventh morning *the seventh morning running*, against a count of five.**
4. **`258:5` called the eighth morning the sixth, called the eighth day the sixth, and said the chalk figure that morning was the same as the figure at the head of it, which was 50,370 against a chalk figure of 50,417.**
5. **`260:85` printed the copy at fourpence against three at `259:73` and three in canon.** Also in this file's own §5 item 5.
6. **`256:3` said the figure had gone up five when it had gone up eight, and said *the fifth of the five was a stranger*, which is not English.**
7. **Seven sites of day-drift: two *a week*s on the fifth day, four *nine days* on the fifth and the seventh, *the second hour of the fifth day* inside the seventh-day chapter, and *nine days* three times on the tenth day of a chapter that says *the first ten days* two lines later.**
8. **`254:15` said a room *four hundred and some weeks away*.**
9. **The hand-off prompt pointed at ten cards that were not on disk, and the prompt also told the Batch 0002 writer it might spend a second panel, which the volume's register does not allow before Batch 0003.** **Both are now true statements: the cards are written and are at the foot of `outline/volume-06.md` under `## BATCH 0002`, and the panel register says no panel in that band.**

**AND SIX MORE FOUND WHILE REPAIRING, of the same class and none of them in the review: `252:23` put a boundary walk on the wrong day of the week; `251:49` said nine days had passed in the first chapter; `253:5` put five rings in the night that were rung between the second light and the noon bell; `state/character-state.md` said *nine days* of chalk against a count of five; and six state-file citations pointed at a `---` line or a line that does not exist.** **THE INSTRUMENT THAT FOUND ALL SIX IS THE SAME ONE THAT FOUND THE REVIEW'S OWN FOUR, AND IT IS THE ONE THIS PROJECT KEEPS REBUILDING: put the number next to the thing it counts and read the two side by side. Nine of the review's findings were in the numeric spine, and the prose it did not find fault with is the prose that was not a number.**

## VOLUME 05 IS CLOSED — the volume-level summary, Chapters 201 to 250, *The Red Basin*. **The live block. Everything below it, from `## VOLUME 05 BATCH 0005 IS DELIVERED` down, is archive and is not edited by this phase.** Chapters 1–250 are canon; Volume 04 is closed, audited and repaired; `outline/series.md` outranks `outline/volume-05.md` and this file. **This is one block and it is the volume's receipt, not a fifth batch, and it resolves nothing.**

**THE RUN: Shelf day seventy-one to one hundred and twenty — the second day of the fiftieth week to the second day of the fifty-seventh — which is basin day one hundred and forty-nine to one hundred and ninety-eight since the second shaking, on the formula basin day = Shelf day + 78, both ends checked: 71 + 78 = 149 and 120 + 78 = 198. Fifty chapters in five batches of ten. He is in the valley fifty days and forty-nine nights. Eighteen day-count figures, three panels, six bold lines and no emphasis spans, forty-four italic spans, ninety-nine day-phrases, six people who arrived on a day nobody fixed, one hundred and forty-eight findings, and one position beneath the basin that did not move.**

### WHAT THE FIVE BATCHES EACH PAID, ONCE, AND NOT REPEATED

**BATCH 0001, CHAPTERS 201–210, *The Red Basin*:** the ground, the water and the three clocks, and the finding that none of them converts into any other; the road that does not arrive where it set out; a deed that is an agreement between two papers; the burden-holder's wage; a bond whose money stopped and whose thing did not; eleven houses that are not a category; a height that means two different things four miles apart; **the quick ruling, four complete lines with no hole in any of them**; three rulings of the chamber in nine years; the first missing anchor position; the new question written once and not asked. *Paid and not paid: paid the arrival, the calendar and the failure of the quick ruling as a thing; not paid the flood, and the flood was not to be paid before Chapter 211.*

**BATCH 0002, CHAPTERS 211–220, *The Four Pages, And The Water*:** the order obeyed exactly and the water arriving; **the midpoint, in three chapters, and the arithmetic of it is one subtraction a reader can do in their head**; nineteen houses and not eleven; nobody drowned and no mitigation written; nobody in this valley is a villain and the bond's promise was kept exactly by the party it names; the four pages stay on the wall; his name is on nothing; the money is not the difficulty; a letter of two paragraphs from a clerk who did his job properly; and a chapter that ends on a person doing their work.

**BATCH 0003, CHAPTERS 221–230, *After The Water, And The Ordinary People*:** **the power progression, spent — *Ilyan must let ordinary witnesses revise his remedy after his first failure* — and it is eleven hands that are not his in eleven places and not in a room**; the list of what nineteen people need; a man who lost a house is not a house; a paper with a man's name on it is read out in a year when he is gone; five objections to his draft and not one of them by a man from four hundred miles off; their version in six lines with three hands on a struck line that do not agree; the settlement costed at £178 13s 4s a year; the first year at £195 7s 4s; and the thing the sheet does not say.

**BATCH 0004, CHAPTERS 231–240, *The Sealed Column*:** **a document in a valley used rather than observed, for the first time in the volume, and the reader is the copy and the copy has no person on it**; a paper kept in a lane by a man who cannot read it with no second copy; the tradesmen price a route that does not go through Orison and there is no gate on it; the word that means not sent said out loud in an ordinary mouth; four lines, all four about this basin, and thirty-two papers that went nine days up a road and were never answered; **being afraid is not a matter**; a good name is a public thing and it is free; the batch's reversal, which is not a flood and has no villain in it; **and the excess of the claims over the whole of the trade is exactly the settlement, to the penny, with nothing behind it**; and a clerk of forty-four saying to a shelf that somebody is going to open that column and it is not going to be me.

**BATCH 0005, CHAPTERS 241–250, *The Redacted Archive, And A Rule-Free Alternative*:** **the volume's climax and its resolution.** What a valley does with a piece of knowledge it cannot act on; the price of asking a chamber a second time and the reason nobody has paid it, which is not the money; the sixty tons turned from a scandal into a question by a person of the valley; the standing of a valley is a document and not a person; **the column opened, by him, in a room, with no authority, out loud, in front of about nine people, nobody having asked him, at the cost of a bench of four's good name and with nobody thanked and nobody relieved**; **Sable Venn's rule-free alternative, offered well, not defeated, not redeemed, and the incompleteness of it named by an ordinary person and not by him**; the figure in the return of the water at the frame; the four people who keep this valley's record reading nine years of it out loud to each other; the mine's standing interest in a number settled in public at four days a year of a pitman's time; **and a revocable charter on a wall with no name on it and no dash on it, the eleven feet in a third document in three copies in three places with a difference of four feet written down in it, an exit review with a date on it, a position in a bearing and a depth and a page, and nobody went down.**

### WHAT THE VOLUME AS A WHOLE PAID

**THE THESIS, IN ONE SENTENCE, AND IT IS THE SERIES'S OWN SENTENCE AND NOT A NEW ONE: earthquakes and contradictory deeds in the Red Basin make it impossible for residents to prove which land charter governs them, and what fifty chapters of that did was replace the proving with a date.** The basin cannot prove which charter governs it. What it has instead is a charter that says in its own first line that it can be ended, a third document in three places that writes down the disagreement instead of settling it, a settlement nobody is on, four pages on a wall that are still correct on their face, a mine that is a mine, and a review anybody can bring the charter to on a day anybody can name. **That is not a resolution of the series' question and it is not meant to be; it is the largest thing fifty chapters could honestly do in a valley of four hundred and ten people with a press in one room and a clerk in it, and the woman of about forty who washes the pen said the reason out loud — there is one clerk in this room who knows what a document is and that is exactly one too few already.**

**AND WHAT THE VOLUME DID TO ITS OWN CHARACTER, in one sentence: Ilyan came with a plan for a quick ruling, the quick ruling failed in the only way a complete document can fail, and what he did next was not a better ruling but a paper written in other people's hands and a name spent that was not his.**

**AND THE CENTRAL PRESSURE, which was never a villain and never a conspiracy: about thirty clerks in a building with a yard at Orison, doing their jobs properly, four hundred miles off, right three times in nine years, with a fee and a nine-day ride between them and a village of four hundred and ten people who could not prove a thing. The Red Basin's disputes were not settled by a conspiracy. They were settled, partly, by a fee and a nine-day ride, and one of them was settled at the end of Chapter 245 by a man opening a bag.**

### THE TWO THINGS THE VOLUME DID NOT PAY, AND A CLOSE DOES NOT PAY THEM NOW

1. **THE STONE.** Four hundred and six feet is the pit and is not the void, and the way down is a chamber with water in it, and the last hundred and forty feet of the water has been under to the roof since before a woman of twenty-six was seventeen, and **nobody went down and nobody stands in front of it.** Anchor 8 remains missing beneath the Red Basin until Volume 14, and that is the series outline's own sentence.
2. **THE SECOND HALF OF ITS OWN QUESTION.** *Where is the missing anchor, and why does it answer from beneath Orison* was not asked out loud in any of the fifty chapters and is not even a whisper, as a panel, as a rendering, as a voice, or as a person remarking that anything answered, and the System put nothing up in fifty chapters. **The first half was produced as a position in Chapter 209 and has not moved. The second half is not this volume's and is not going to be anybody's until a volume that is written to carry it.**

### THE LIST OF THE THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM, AND IT IS THE LIST THIS PROJECT BUILT ONE FAILURE AT A TIME

1. **A count in a state file that carries no line number beside it is unverified, however confident the sentence around it is.** This is the standing rule and it is the whole of what has gone wrong in this project eight times in two hundred and fifty chapters. **Re-run the grep against the chapter files and do not re-read the list.**
2. **THE DAY-PHRASE SWEEP IS A LEGALITY CHECK AND NOT A TRUTH CHECK.** It returned zero illegal across ninety-nine phrases in this volume and the truth question found two chapter dates that conflict with the volume's own table, at `231:3` and `231:7`, and at `238:3` and `238:45`. **The cheapest instrument is fifty chapter days against ninety-nine day-phrases, read side by side, and it takes ten minutes.**
3. **A BAND FIGURE MEASURED BY A PERSON IS A FIGURE WITH NO MEASUREMENT IN IT.** Two of the five band figures in the blocks below this one were wrong: Batch 0003 printed twenty-eight and is fourteen, which is exactly double and is therefore a doubled count, and Batch 0005 printed nineteen and is twenty-two. Batches 0001, 0002 and 0004 printed 11, 26 and 26 and are right.
4. **THE DAY-COUNT IS SPELLED OUT IN WORDS IN EVERY ONE OF ITS EIGHTEEN INSTANCES AND A SCAN FOR THREE DIGITS RETURNS ZERO.** Measure the words. And the count is a count of instances in Chapters 201 to 250 and is **not** added to Volume 04's twenty-two, which is a count of instances in Chapters 151 to 200 of a different thing in a different volume.
5. **THE PANEL COUNT IS THREE AND IT IS A COUNT OF PANELS AND NOT A COUNT OF LINES, AND THE BOLD INVENTORY IS SIX LINES AND ZERO OF THEM ARE EMPHASIS.** A count printed without that distinction is a count of a thing whose unit is not stated.
6. **THE DUPLICATE SCAN IS A SPAN SCAN AND NOT A LINE SCAN, AND THE UNIT MUST BE DECLARED.** A line in these files is a whole paragraph and a volume that puts eighty-word sentences inside one line will return zero from a line scan forever. At seventy characters, the span instrument returns 4,147 distinct repeated spans and 4,901 repeat occurrences over the fifty chapters; expect this volume's three deliberate formulae to dominate it, and read what is left, because what is left is the only useful part, and what is left here is nothing that is not a formula.
7. **A MONTH-WORD SCAN RETURNS FORTY-ONE HITS AND FORTY OF THEM ARE THE MODAL VERB *MAY*, INCLUDING THREE INSIDE ALL-CAPITALS BLOCK QUOTATIONS, AND THE ONE THAT IS NOT IS A PROPER NOUN.** Subtract the modal before believing either figure. The weekday pattern this project records as plural-safe has no plural on its Monday alternative and cannot see a Monday at all; run `\b(mondays?|tuesdays?|wednesdays?|thursdays?|fridays?|saturdays?|sundays?)\b`.
8. **AN ITALIC COUNT WITHOUT ITS SPLIT IS A COUNT OF A THING WHOSE UNIT IS NOT STATED.** Forty-four spans: nineteen are his own hand and twenty-five are textual citation.
9. **A MONEY CHAPTER WITH A LONG RUNNING FIGURE IN IT WILL DRIVE THE COUNT OF THE WORD *NINE* UP, AND THE FIX IS NOT TO ABBREVIATE THE FIGURE.** Four hundred and sixty-six whole-word occurrences of *nine* over the fifty chapters and four hundred and forty-eight of *eleven*, and the residue in both is real quantities in the fiction and not filler.
10. **A SCRIPT CHECKS THE FIGURES IT IS GIVEN AND DOES NOT READ THE SENTENCE THEY SIT IN.** This volume was bitten by that in three reviews running: a unit price converted wrongly, a chain of three relations that could not yield the figure printed at the end of it, a ratio stated in round words, and a number that contradicted the sentence beside it. **Print the unit price in the sentence, find it, multiply, and then read the sentence.**
11. **THE FIGURE OF FOUR HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-FIVE POUNDS IS NAMED IN FIVE CHAPTERS AND NOT IN ONE, AND THE RECORD SAID ONE FOR ELEVEN MONTHS.** `206:101`, `220:45`, `230:35`, `239:63`, `250:47`. **A close may name it again in a hand and may not say that the year turns.** The Batch 0005 exception — Chapter 250 naming it once, in a hand, in a book in a cart shed, in nobody else's mouth — was spent by Chapter 250 and is recorded as spent.
12. **AN ANONYMITY IS A DECISION AND NOT AN OMISSION, AND SO IS AN ABSENCE.** His name is on nothing in the valley and the space at the foot of the settlement is neither filled nor dashed; the charter carries no name and no dash; and the height is not on the settlement and is in a third document in three copies with the disagreement written in it. **None of the three may be tidied.**
13. **A DOCUMENT THAT IS BETTER THAN THE THING IT REPLACES IS STILL THE WRONG SHAPE, AND ITS PRICE IS A ROOF.** The charter is worth the roofs and is not worth the wage, because a paper that can be ended cannot hold a wage, and the figure it cannot hold is the wage and a woman of thirty-eight said so out loud before she was asked.
14. **NOBODY IS RELIEVED BY A CLOSE RECORD.** The carrying is off nothing; the blank is a blank; the second notice is unpaid; the claim is unproducible and the man is right; the dead man is dead; the letter is unopened; Nesh Arrad's letter has not arrived and cannot have arrived; and the volume ends on a person doing their work, which is not a resolution.
15. **A STATE FILE THAT REPORTS ITS OWN REPAIRS IS A STATE FILE THAT HAS TO BE CHECKED AGAINST THE CHAPTER FILE AND NOT AGAINST THE INTENTION.** A sweep cannot see an intention, and twice in this project a record said a sentence had been taken out and it had not.

### AND ONE SENTENCE ABOUT THE COST OF BEING RIGHT, because this block is the seventh of its kind and the shape of it is the finding

**`state/current.md` grew by about ninety-six kilobytes in this close and recorded four things the record below it had wrong, and the finding is the one the Volume 04 close recorded and has not improved: a repair pass costs almost as much as it saves, and a record that grows while it corrects itself is a record that is going to be a problem in three more volumes. A phase with its own prompt should prune by DELETING A WHOLE SUPERSEDED GENERATION and not by editing inside an archive block. The three generations of Volume 03 Timeline / Active threats / Progress blocks are the obvious candidate, one per batch, each already superseded by the one above it. Nothing in this close deleted anything and nothing in this close should have.**

## VOLUME 05 BATCH 0005 IS DELIVERED — Chapters 241–250, *The Redacted Archive, And A Rule-Free Alternative*. **The live block. Everything below is archive and is not edited by this phase.**

**Batch goal, as the volume outline fixes it and as this batch received it: the climax. He opens the redacted archive to the basin rather than protect the Bench's reputation, and Sable Venn arrives and offers a rule-free alternative, and he cannot prove her wrong, only incomplete. The resolution: a revocable local charter and an exit review; the first missing anchor position identified beneath the Red Basin, its stone not reached and nobody standing in front of it.**

**The batch's shape in one line: a valley of about four hundred and ten people has known for a week that a record about their water exists four hundred miles away and was not sent to it, and in ten days it finds out what to do about it, which is: find out what it would cost, find out who may speak, decide not to send anybody, and then have a man from four hundred miles off open the page in a room, because the four people it belongs to cannot be asked anything and nine people in the valley were being crushed by a thing that costs those four people nothing at all.**

### WHAT THE BATCH PAID, ONCE EACH, WITH THE CHAPTER

1. **A valley's answer to a thing it cannot act on is to go on doing the work, and it is both a decision and a habit** — 241.
2. **The cheap way to a withheld record is to ask somebody who has been there, and there is nobody, and it is established in a cart yard in about four minutes** — 241.
3. **The question is not *how do we get it* and it is *what would we do with it if we had it*, and it is the most expensive sentence anybody has said in this valley in a month** — 241.
4. **Being frightened has a shape and the shape is a valley of ninety houses where everybody knows which house everybody lives in** — 241.
5. **The price of asking a chamber for one thing is £5 6s and the reason nobody has paid it is not the price** — 242.
6. **A second paper puts a second date on a matter, and a second date is a thing a bench can use to close a first one, and a bench that is seized of a matter and is doing its job properly will do it** — 242.
7. **A mine pays the £5 6s out of its own road cost, says out loud that it is buying a date and not a conscience, and is not thanked and is not stopped** — 242.
8. **The sixty tons is two people's arithmetic and not sixty tons of anything, in nine words from a man who has counted carts for nineteen years** — 243.
9. **A figure four years old cannot be reconciled to a memory of nineteen years by anybody in the valley, and the man who says so is a person of the valley and not a clerk from Orison** — 243.
10. **A man of sixty-one has counted what goes into his own pans every day for thirty years and has never once been asked what comes up off a road** — 243.
11. **The first time in about a month that a number in this valley has got smaller rather than larger, and nobody thanked anybody for it** — 243.
12. **The standing of a valley is not in a person but in a document, and eleven people can end it in a road with one sentence** — 244.
13. **A paper nobody is on cannot be asked anybody anything, said by a man of thirty-four who cannot read the paper it is about and who has been the copy for a month** — 244.
14. **THE COLUMN IS OPENED, by him, in a room, with no authority, out loud, in front of about nine people, and nobody had asked him** — 245.
15. **What makes it a choice and not an accident: he could have gone up the hill and nobody in four hundred and ten would ever have found out, and what he had instead was nothing whatever** — 245.
16. **He could have said the sentence that splits the thirty from the four and did not, and says so in his own words, because the sentence that separates them is the sentence that protects the institution** — 245.
17. **A record can be perfectly kept and still be wrong about a place, and the reason is that nobody who kept it was ever in it — said once by a man of thirty-one about a building and again by a man of fifty-two about four people in a room, and the second is better and is in a worse voice** — 245, 248.
18. **NOBODY HAS HIDDEN ANYTHING, said a second time, in the room, where it costs somebody something the first time it did not** — 245.
19. **Nobody thanked him and nobody was relieved, and a bench of four's good name is a public thing and does not survive the asking** — 245.
20. **SABLE VENN ARRIVES, on foot, from the north, with a board, and refuses the sheet on the wall before it is offered** — 246.
21. **Her alternative is genuinely better and genuinely worse, and the person it is worse for says so herself, at her own gate, before she is asked, with the working** — 246.
22. **The incompleteness is named by an ordinary person in the room and not by him: a rule-free valley cannot be given anything, and the honest name for a person in that position is a person who is not in the instruments** — 246.
23. **He had the standing to say the sentence he learned once in another county and did not say it, and the reason is printed and is not a rule** — 246.
24. **She did not win and did not lose, the paper is not hers and she is not on it, and about four people in that room will remember and about six will not** — 246.
25. **The figure in the return of the water at the frame reaches the woman it is about, read out in a lane by a man who cannot read, and she does not celebrate and says something ordinary and true** — 247.
26. **A return of a river is a thing that already happened and a height is a thing you decide to be above, and this valley had never been told what the water does** — 247.
27. **The four people who keep this valley's record read nine years of it out loud to each other in an hour and find that there is no column in any of it for what this valley trades** — 248.
28. **The mine's standing interest in a number is settled in public, and what it costs the mine is not six shillings and eightpence a year but the right to say the chamber got this valley wrong** — 248.
29. **THE ELEVEN FEET GOES INTO A THIRD DOCUMENT IN THREE COPIES IN THREE PLACES, both sills named, the difference of four feet written down, on the strength of the chamber's own second ruling, and it is the worst answer and it is the right one** — 249.
30. **A bad answer in three copies can be argued with by three people and a good answer in one can be argued with by nobody** — 249, in a joiner's mouth, and it is the volume's last civic finding.
31. **THE REVOCABLE LOCAL CHARTER, on a wall, in one hand, with no name on it and no dash on it, and a person may leave it without losing anything but the charter, and it is worse than the ruling he proposed and better than the flood** — 249.
32. **A paper that can be ended cannot hold a wage, because a wage is the only one of these things that is still going when the roofs are done, and the whole of what it cannot hold is 1,840 pence** — 249, said by the woman it is about, before she was asked.
33. **About nine people who were afraid of a good name are not afraid of it any more, and it is not because anybody was made safe** — 249.
34. **The exit review has a date on it and any person of this valley may bring it** — 249, 250.
35. **THE POSITION BENEATH THE BASIN IS A BEARING AND A DEPTH AND A PAGE NUMBER, it has not moved, the way down is a chamber with water in it, four hundred and six feet is the pit and is not the void, and NOBODY WENT DOWN** — 250.
36. **The leg is used once in fifty chapters, in the passage where he puts down nothing, as a fact about a wall** — 250.
37. **The volume ends on a person doing their work** — 250.

### THE MONEY, with the working printed beside every figure, and a script that checked all twenty-eight of them and returned zero failures

**The matter to Orison, £5 6s, SPENT IN THIS BATCH FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THE VOLUME, by the Anner out of the Anner's own cost of the high road: 240d + 960d + 72d = 1,272d.** Against the wages of this valley: **ten weeks of a pitman's pay and 72d over; eleven weeks of a sluice-keeper's pay and 84d over, which is about three quarters of the sixteen weeks a year she is paid for.** Against the settlement: **1,272 × 33 = 41,976d and 42,880 − 41,976 = 904d over, so thirty-three whole times.** The sixty tons: **60 × £7 = £420 a year; 60 tons = 1,200 hundredweights; £1 4s = 288d, so 1,200 × 288d = 345,600d = £1,440; and 100,800 × 100 ÷ 1,747,200 = 5.77, five and seven-tenths in the hundred, and £420 against the settlement is 100,800 ÷ 42,880 = 2.35, two and a third times.** The sluice-keeper's pit half: **16 × 54d = 864d = £3 12s a year.** The cost of the mine being bound in advance: **120 ÷ 6 = 20d a day, four of them = 80d = 6s 8d, and 80 × 100 ÷ 6,000 = 1.33, one and a third in the hundred.** The third document: **1d + 1d + 3d = 5d.** The charter: **a roof is £9 = 2,160d, nineteen of them is 41,040d = £171 exactly, the settlement is 42,880d, the difference is 1,840d = £7 13s 4d, and 1,840d is the whole wage plus the penny a day — and 41,040d is also what the flood cost, to the penny.** The two sills, under the geometry ruled by the review of 27 September 2026: **the frame's sill is 108in ABOVE the Anner's, so 108in + 28in = 136in = 11ft 4in, four inches over the bond's line; and the bed is 13ft below the frame's sill and 4ft below the Anner's sill, so two lines that are both eleven feet are 4ft — 48in — apart, which is the four feet the third document is about. The chain 9 + 4 = 13, 13 − 11 = 2, 2 + 2 = 4 that stood here until 27 September 2026 was a non-sequitur.** Debts, named once, at `250:47` and `250:49`, and not converted: **£485 on £9,700 = 9,700 × 12d = 116,400d ✓; 5s 4d ✓; £45 2s 8d ✓.** **AND THE FORTY-FIVE TIMES COMPARISON IS NOT RESTAGED AND FORTY-FIVE IS NOT USED AS A MULTIPLIER ANYWHERE IN VOLUME 05.**

7. **A wrong cost for the opening in Chapter 245**: four pence for four lines on one sheet, and a sheet is one page and a page is a penny.

11. **A contradiction inside one chapter** about how far apart two papers on one wall are, and it is now a hand's breadth from four pages in one place and about a foot in another, and the two are different walls.
12. **A tangled chain of arithmetic in Chapter 249** that did not finish, now printed in full and in order with the finding at the end of it.

### THE TWO THINGS THIS BATCH ADDS TO THE PROJECT'S OWN LIST OF WHAT A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE IT

1. **THE CROSS-BATCH DUPLICATE SCAN IS A DIFFERENT INSTRUMENT FROM THE WITHIN-BATCH ONE AND ONLY THE CROSS-BATCH ONE CATCHES A CARRY-OVER.** Both return zero over Chapters 241–250 at forty characters and above, and a chapter can pass both and still have lifted a line out of Chapter 233. **Run it on every batch. It costs one command.**
2. **A BATCH PROMPT CAN BE WRONG ABOUT A CHARACTER AND THE CHAPTER FILE IS THE FACT.** The Batch 0005 card for Chapter 250 says a man of twenty-six at the pit in a sentence about her, and the prompt's own preamble and the repaired Chapter 240 both establish a woman of twenty-six. **This is the second time in two batches that a hand-off document has been wrong about her, and the state file was the thing that was wrong the first time.**

### THE THING THIS BATCH DID NOT DO

**It did not write a review, and it did not write a repair, and it did not write a sixth batch, and it did not write a volume audit, and it did not write a `.done` marker, and it did not touch `state/phase-ledger.json`.** **It did not resolve a thread to make the batch land. Every one of the eighteen threads came out of it exactly as it went in except the column, which this batch opens because the outline says Chapter 245 or 246 and it is the volume's climax and not a thing a batch may postpone for tidiness. The carrying is off nothing. The blank is a blank. Nobody is redeemed. Nobody is forgiven. The name stays off the eleven-hand sheet and the space at its foot stays empty and undashed. The eleven feet goes into a third document in three copies and not onto the settlement. And the mine is a mine.** **THE NEXT PHASE IS THE VOLUME 05 CLOSE AND ITS PROMPT IS AT `workspace/volume-05/volume-close/PROMPT.md`.**
