**Batch goal, as the volume outline fixes it and as this batch received it: the pressure toward the Bench's reputation; the chamber's own index at Orison carries a column whose heading is a word that means not sent, and the word is named in an ordinary mouth by a person who has to look at the heading every day; nobody in this movement opens it, asks for it, or is asked about it, and the climax is that HE opens it; Sable Venn is named twice more and at a distance and does not arrive; the Red Basin's own tradesmen begin to organise a route that does not go through Orison, and that is the shape of Volume 06 and it is named once and not followed.**

**The batch's shape in one line: a valley of about four hundred and ten people learns in one week that a record about its water exists four hundred miles away and was not sent to it, and the whole of the batch is what it does about that, which is go on doing the work, and the only person in it who could do anything else is a man who is carrying a matter for a district four hundred miles off and has no standing and has no form, and who put his hand on a table about a foot from the answer on the sixth day of the week and took it away again.**

### WHAT THE BATCH PAID, ONCE EACH, WITH THE CHAPTER

A document administered by being read aloud, and a copy kept in a lane by a man who cannot read it that has no second copy (231). A route that does not go through Orison, named once in a cart yard and not followed, and the water-seat being the reason there is a seat (232). **The column named twice in an ordinary mouth, and four lines under it, all four about this basin, and nobody asking, and the reason a hand-copy of what was sent is empty and the original is not (233).** Nobody asking, and the basin's answer being to go on doing the work, and a roofer of fifty-six asking the first question anybody here has asked the man of thirty-one about what he is carrying (234). **The chamber's own return, consistent with itself and wrong about the place, the only figure anybody anywhere has written down about what this valley trades, and the discovery that a thing true in every room and absent from every page is a thing that has no owner (235).** Nine people frightened of a bench of four they have never met, and a good name being a public thing and it is free (236). A name travelling four miles on about four mouths with no document under it (237). **The batch's reversal, done by the basin's own tradesmen for a good reason, with nobody a villain in it and a cart not where it was, and a paper four hundred and ten people had never read all the way through depending on where a cart goes (238).** And the arithmetic, in which the settlement and a second road are the same money counted once and the excess over the whole of the trade is exactly the settlement, to the penny, with nothing behind the difference (239). And the closing sentence: **somebody is going to open that column, and it is not going to be me** (240).

### THE MONEY, WITH THE WORKING PRINTED BESIDE EVERY FIGURE

### THE SIXTEEN THINGS THIS BATCH'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, ALL CAUGHT BY THE PROJECT'S OWN INSTRUMENTS, ALL CORRECTED IN THE PROSE BEFORE DELIVERY

1. **One weekday.** The word *Tuesday* in Chapter 235, in a sentence reaching for a stock phrase. Out. **The second weekday in three batches of this volume, and the pattern that catches it is the one the Volume 04 audit found this project was structurally incapable of running.**
2. **Five meta-narration sentences** at `233:39`, `234:41`, `235:79`, `236:59`, `237:45`. All out. **The batch's cards said *the finding is* eleven times and the prose caught five of them.**
3. **Seven bold markers**, one of them a bold heading inside the block-quoted document in Chapter 233 and six emphasis spans in Chapters 231 and 236. All out. The emphasis count is now zero.
4. **Two italicised spoken phrases** at `231:27` and `236:37`. Now quotation marks. **Seven italic spans remain and all seven are textual citation.**
5. **A line number in the prose** at Chapter 236, citing `227:15`. Out, and replaced with the fact.
6. **A wrong date inside a legal band** at `237:47`. Caught by the third question, not by the pattern.
7. **A village's figure used for a valley** at Chapter 234: *about four hundred and six people in this valley*, where Hollow Sallow is about four hundred and ten and the basin is not a village. Out.
8. **A count that did not add up between two chapters** — *three places* in Chapter 234 against four in Chapter 236. Now *more places than I can count inside a week*.
9. **A garbled subtraction** in Chapter 234's money paragraph: *a pound, four hundred and sixty pence for the two days*. **The working now reads 240 + 960 + 72 = 1,272d = £5 6s.**
10. **A wrong penny figure and a wrong percentage in Chapter 235**: *a hundred and eight thousand pence*, which is 108,000d and not 100,800d, and the 6.18 that came out of it. **Now one hundred thousand eight hundred pence and five and seven-tenths in the hundred, with the multiplication printed in the sentence.** The fourth time in this volume that a figure has been wrong in a state-shaped sentence.
11. **The leg in two chapters instead of one.** It was in Chapter 233 in the first writing. Cut from 233. One use in a batch is the rule and two is a refrain.
12. **The name named four times instead of twice** in Chapter 237. Now twice, once from each of two people who have never met.
13. **A first-person verb in a third-person narration** at Chapter 233: *has paid for every one of them since I came*, in a narrator's sentence. Now *without once being asked to*, and the clerk's first person is kept inside his own speech.
14. **A wrong unit in a division** at Chapter 239: *two hundred and forty pounds goes into that*. Now *two hundred and forty pence to the pound goes into that*, **and the whole chapter was then re-checked line by line against a script and all twenty-one of its figures are right.**
15. **A nonsense fraction and a miscounted interval** at Chapter 239: *fourteen out of a hundred and eighty-seven*, and *five days between the last one and this one* where 187 − 183 is **three**. **178 to 183 is four and `235:53` prints four; 183 to 187 is three and `239:59` prints three.**
16. **The word NINE at one hundred and fifty-eight** against a trend of ninety-eight, eighty-eight and eighty-four. Fifty-three cut, and the residue is explained in the instruments section above rather than hidden.

### THE TWO THINGS THIS BATCH ADDED TO THE PROJECT'S OWN LIST OF WHAT A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE IT

1. **A money chapter with a long running figure in it will drive the count of the word NINE up, and the fix is NOT to abbreviate the figure.** Chapter 239 prints *nine hundred and ninety-eight thousand four hundred* twelve times because the number being subtracted from has to be printed every time it is used, and *two hundred and forty-nine thousand six hundred* four more, and every one of those is a place a reader can check. **A batch that sees the count of the word at one hundred and five against a trend of eighty-four and reaches for the digits has made the money uncheckable, and the direction of that trade is always the wrong way.** Print the count, print the reason, and leave the arithmetic alone.
2. **A scan for a day-count in digits returns zero in this volume and always will, because the volume's instances are spelled out in the prose, and a state file that certifies a day-count at nothing because it ran a digits scan has certified nothing.** Measure the words. **And the complement of that: a day-phrase sweep is a legality check, not a truth check, and the cheapest second instrument for a calendar is to put every chapter's own day next to every day-phrase in it and read the two lists side by side, which is ten rows against twenty-five and takes a minute, and it found a defect in this batch that the pattern was structurally incapable of seeing.**

### THE THING THIS BATCH DID NOT DO, AND IT IS THE WHOLE OF IT

**It did not open the column, it did not ask for it, and it did not let anybody ask him when he is going to.** The column is named twice and shut, in a batch whose subject is the knowledge that it is there, and the opening is Chapter 245 or 246 and belongs to Batch 0005, and a batch that opens it before then spends the volume's climax on a chapter card. Nobody turned the year. The new question is not raised and the Shelf half of the book is not opened. His name is not back on the settlement and the space is not filled and is not dashed. The height is not on the paper. The carrying is off nothing. The four hundred and eighty-five pounds does not stop. Nobody is redeemed, nobody is forgiven, and about nine people in a valley of four hundred and ten know something they can do nothing with and are not relieved.

## VOLUME 05 BATCH 0003 IS DELIVERED — Chapters 221–230, *After The Water, And The Ordinary People*. **The live block. Everything below it is archive and is not edited by this phase.**

**Batch goal, as `outline/series.md` fixes it and verbatim: *Burden Bearer; Ilyan must let ordinary witnesses revise his remedy after his first failure.*** **Batch goal as the series' own card for 221–230 adds: the remedy is not withdrawn by him; it is rewritten in eleven hands that are not his; and the settlement is worse for him than the one he proposed.**

**The batch's shape in one line: the four pages held a dam to a number and put water in nineteen houses, and this batch is about what eleven ordinary people write down when they are asked to put it right, and the answer is a sheet of six lines that names no person and no height, and it works, and it works because the mine pays, and the mine now has a standing interest in a number that put water in nineteen houses, and that interest is the batch's cost and it does not go away when the roofs are on.**

### WHAT THE BATCH PAID, ONCE EACH, WITH THE CHAPTER

The order of the work and the finding that eighteen of nineteen answers cannot be bought (221). The question of naming houses, and the answer, and a list with addresses argued against the wrong door at a ninth doorway (222). The money in full with the working, and the finding that what the mine can pay is nothing (223). The wage that comes every week, the three years paid in an envelope, and a second hand on a hundred-year-old book at a penny a day (224). A man who lost a house, eight days of nobody asking him, and a paper with no names on it (225). His draft, block-quoted, and five objections, all by people it names or does not name (226). **The eleven hands, the name out of the document, the panel, and a clerk counting eleven out loud so that six people who had gone quiet would know it was not the eleven of anything** (227). Their sheet, block-quoted, read aloud by a man who cannot read it, two words changed and a line added (`228`). The first year costing more than the flood (`229`). And a gate hung, and the height left off (`230`).

### THE MONEY, WITH THE WORKING PRINTED BESIDE EVERY FIGURE

£171 for nineteen roofs at £9 (2,160d × 19 = 41,040d; ÷240 = 171 ✓). £7 4s for the whole wage (16 × 108d = 1,728d ✓). 9s 4s for the second hand (16 × 7 = 112 days; 112 ÷ 12 = 9s 4d ✓). **£178 13s 4s a year recurring (41,040 + 1,728 + 112 = 42,880d; −42,720 = 160d ✓).** £5 18s for the gate and £10 16s of arrears, £16 14s once (1,416 + 2,592 = 4,008d; ÷240 = 16r 168d; 168 ÷ 12 = 14s ✓). **£195 7s 4d in the first year (42,880 + 4,008 = 46,888d; −46,800 = 88d ✓).** **£24 7s 4d more than the flood (46,888 − 41,040 = 5,848d; ÷240 = 24r 88d ✓).** **Three years and about six weeks of a sluice-keeper's paid year (5,848 ÷ 1,728 = 3.38; 664d ÷ 108d = 6.15 ✓).** A hair under one part in eight of the whole thing not roofs (5,848 × 8 = 46,784; 46,888 − 46,784 = 104d ✓). Two and three-tenths in the hundred of the £7,700 road (42,880 ÷ 1,848,000 = 0.0232 ✓). Nine shillings and fourpence is about a fifteenth of the wage (1,728 ÷ 112 = 15.4 ✓). Five pence for two pages and a seal (2d + 3d ✓). £5 6s for a matter to Orison, **not spent twice** (1,272d ✓). **AND THE FIGURES THAT TRAVEL AS DEBTS AND NOT AS PRICES, ALL SIX NAMED ONCE IN ONE SENTENCE AND NONE ADVANCED: £485 a year on £9,700, the second notice of 5s 4d, the claim of £45 2s 8d, ninety-one numbered sheets, about nine hundred and forty forms, the minute of the forty-eighth week, the dead man in the fourth line, and the unopened letter of a clerk of thirty-four.**

### THE TEN THINGS THIS BATCH'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, ALL CAUGHT BY THE PROJECT'S OWN INSTRUMENTS, ALL CORRECTED IN THE PROSE BEFORE DELIVERY

The working won in the outline and in the prose.** This is the fifth time in this volume that a rounder figure beat the parts.
2. **Two intervals of eleven days where the interval is eight: `225:3` and `224:47`. The counting was at basin 165 and the refusal at basin 173.**

10. **Two stock numbers used as filler, *about eleven minutes* and *about eleven hours*, the second of them an echo of the eleven hands in the same paragraph.**

### THE TWO THINGS THIS BATCH ADDED TO THE PROJECT'S OWN LIST OF WHAT A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE IT

1. **TWENTY-NINE DAYS IN THE VALLEY BY CHAPTER 230, NOT NINETEEN AND NOT THIRTY.** 178 − 149 = **29**, and the nineteen was right for Chapter 220 and is wrong here, and the same class of error was made and repaired once already in this volume at the review of Batch 0002. **This item's heading said thirty while its own arithmetic said twenty-nine, and that is the fourth time in this project that a heading has been contradicted by the nine words under it.**
2. **A DOCUMENT CAN ACQUIRE A LINE IN A ROAD AND NOBODY WITH A SEAT CAN DO ANYTHING ABOUT IT, AND THE REASON IS WHOSE COPY IT IS.** A clerk of forty-four has said it and nobody has answered him, and the answer is not coming in this volume, and the seventh line of the sheet exists only in a lane.

**AND THE THING THIS BATCH DELIBERATELY DID NOT DO, WHICH IS THE WHOLE OF ITS MORAL POSITION: it did not turn the Shelf's grain year, did not put the four hundred and eighty-five pounds into a basin's mouth as a figure of the basin, did not name the fourth column, did not name Sable Venn, did not open anything, did not put the height on the sheet, did not put his name back, did not thank anybody, forgive anybody, redeem anybody, or resolve one of the fourteen threads, and did not write a review of itself.**

### WHAT THIS PHASE WAS, AND WHAT IT PRODUCED

**It was the Volume 05 BATCH 0002 phase and it did three things in one run, in that order: it extended `outline/volume-05.md` with a Batch 0002 table, a day-count table, a panel line, a block-quote table and a money table; it wrote Chapters 211 to 220; and it wrote the state-file blocks. It produced no review, no close, no volume audit and no third-batch prompt, and the prompt for Batch 0003 is not this phase's to write and has not been written.**

### WHAT THE BATCH DELIVERED, once each

**The four pages obeyed exactly and the order's own arithmetic turning against it. A girl of seventeen who asks the first question anybody in that village has ever asked about a document. A second matter to Orison dated on the outside with a day out of a river, because a grain year does not exist in this basin. A standing order that names a weather instead of a day, with no name on it, kept to for three years by a woman who has never been asked what she is writing on the Seat's own book. A man of seventy-four and a joiner and a foot of water, and two careful men who differ by a foot and neither of whom is lying. Three days, and three days is not a figure, and a clerk of a Seat reading a four-line order out in a lane on eleven chairs with nobody signing anything. Eleven feet held exactly, on purpose, with a string and a barrow and most of a morning. Nine words in ochre dust: I have been given a number and never a reason. THE MIDPOINT, in three chapters: the height is held, the water comes up backwards because its somewhere has been taken off it, the gate does its job and the job ends at a frame nine feet below the number, and two feet above the frame's sill is about the height of the floors of the houses on the upper half of that lane. Nobody drowned and that is not a mitigation. Nineteen houses and not eleven, and the eleven were right about the eleven, and the eight are houses nobody marked, and nobody in this valley is a villain and the mine kept the promise. It named a dam, it named a height, it named a day, it never named the water. A rule that names no bystander is carried by the nearest body with hands, said once, in a different sentence, not explained and not asked about. The four pages staying on a wall because a basin with no document is worse, said by a woman with no authority. His name on nothing at all, which is the one thing that has not gone wrong and is not a relief and nobody in the room said so. Nineteen houses at about nine pounds a roof, which is a hundred and seventy-one pounds, which is about twenty-four years of a sluice-keeper's whole year, and she is standing in it. Nineteen people asked what they need, in about nine words each, and not one answer is money, and the list is a document and it is under a stone on a board in a cart shed and he has not read it. A letter of two paragraphs from a clerk who did his job properly, saying the Bench is seized of the matter and will consider it in its own time, at ten pounds and twelve and nine days. Nine items read aloud off the Shelf side of a book on a bank, and a tenth that is not said. And a gate at the closing of the water and a chair put back at a table, with the mud not down.**

It produced no review, no close, no second batch and no prompt of any kind.** The outline exists before the first sentence of Chapter 201 and the outline was not edited afterwards to make the chapters look as though it had.

### WHAT THE BATCH DELIVERED, once each

**The arrival and a road that is not where it set out. One word with two meanings in two margins eighty-two years and nine turns apart, both men telling the truth. A plan of four pages agreed on a road in nine words each, and the closing fact that there is nobody in the basin who can be in the room when true pages are read. Three clocks that do not convert, said once by a man scraping salt. A deed that is two papers, said once by a scrivener while copying. A sluice-keeper's £7 4s a year, half of it owed for three years, and about a hundred and forty days of water a year paid for at ninety, and the causeway she lost nine years back to explain it, and the first question anybody ever asked her asked by the wrong man on purpose. A bond of forty pounds, void for eleven years, with the ink run across the words that matter, and four men with a barrow holding a dam the whole time. Eleven houses that are not a category, and eleven doorpost marks cut in one day with a string, and a joiner's sentence that a height is a height above something. A quick ruling in four lines with no hole in any of them, said out loud by him before anybody worked it out, and not noticed by anybody. A printed abstract of three rulings that nobody in the basin had ever seen, one of which the four pages breach, and a letter nobody opened, and a column with no entries under it. The first missing anchor position, identified by two accurate records that do not meet, four hundred and six feet down and more than that by an unknown quantity, with the water at the low end and nobody going down and the System saying nothing. And the new question, written down once, not asked, and broken off in the middle.**

## BATCH 0004 REPAIR PASS, AFTER THE REVIEW OF THIS BATCH — **NINE FINDINGS, ALL REPAIRED, NO CHAPTER RESTARTED, NO CARD MOVED, NO PLOT CHANGED. A LATER PASS THAT FINDS AN OLD FIGURE IN ONE OF THE FILES BELOW IS READING A FILE THIS BLOCK SUPERSEDES.**

**WHAT THE REVIEW FOUND BLOCKING, AND WHAT IS NOW TRUE.** **THE BATCH 0005 BOARD TABLE DID NOT ADD UP FROM ROW 295 DOWN.** The off-by-one rule says the morning in row *N* is the morning in row *N-1* plus the rings printed in row *N*, and the running line had `63,799 + 8 = 63,813` at row 295, so six consecutive mornings were inflated by eight and the volume's closing figure was eight out. **The six are corrected to 63,807 / 63,813 / 63,817 / 63,825 / 63,833 / 63,841, the ten rings sum to sixty-seven, and 63,774 + 67 = 63,841.** **SO 63,841 IS THE FIGURE AT THE SECOND LIGHT ON CHAPTER 300'S MORNING AND NOT 63,849, and the difference from 63,510 is 331 and not 339, and of that 331 the two hundred still arrived at the start of Batch 0004 and the other 131 is fifty days of a village doing things. Chapter 300's own day of eight is NOT in the table, because the last row's rings are Chapter 299's own day, so the first morning after the volume is 63,849 and that figure is derived in the fiction and printed in no table and in no card.** **CHAPTER 281 SAID ITS OWN DAY THREE WAYS** — a stranger, then four of the clock and three on it, then a day of four — **and the chain needs seven, because 63,714 plus seven is 63,721.** It now says the day was going to be seven and came to seven and it was not the ordinary one. **The two Batch 0004 tables were not wrong; their column headers were**, and both now read *the rings of the day BEFORE this morning, four plus*, the ten own-days are printed on a line of their own, and `state/current.md`'s running line no longer prints `63,710 + 7 = 63,714` with a tick on it. **The ten own-days, which is the only reading of the chain that does not put a chapter's own day on the row before it: 281 = 7, 282 = 7, 283 = 13, 284 = 4, 285 = 4, 286 = 6, 287 = 8, 288 = 4, 289 = 7, 290 = 4, and the ten sum to sixty-four, and 63,710 + 64 = 63,774.**

**THE SEVEN PROSE FIXES, one line each, and no prose was touched except where the fault was in the prose.** `283:5` dated the man of thirty-four's departure to the fourth day of a week whose fourth day is the next chapter and contradicted his own return at `281:71`; it is the second day out and the third day back. `283:101` gave the next morning a ninth stroke on top of its four and `284:3` gave the same morning four bells and nothing on top of them and called the thirteen of the day before the nine; the morning now stands at four with the nine of the day before still on the wall above it, and the woman of about fifty has had one thirteen in eleven weeks and one about four years back, which is what `283:3` has always said. `287:5` said there were not about nine people in the Coldharrow yard at any hour at all and `287:47` put nine of them there at the ninth hour, and `287:51` put that yard nine miles off; line 5 is now scoped to before the ninth hour, line 47 says her own yard, and line 51 says this yard. `290:57` put twenty-two years at a bar inside the mouth of a recordwright of twenty-eight who has never stood at one; it is six years as a recordwright, and the twenty-two years stay with the woman of about fifty at `290:51`. `290:91` dated 63,510 to the first morning of the fortieth day, which is this morning; it is the first morning of the first day of his being in this country, which is what `280:65` has always said. And `290:91` printed the morning figure a second time in the same shape as `290:3`, so that figure has been taken out and the sentence says the board is two hundred and sixty-four higher this morning, which is the same arithmetic and one line instead of two.

**THE TWO RECORD FIXES. THE WITHIN-BATCH FORTY-CHARACTER SPAN COUNT HAS BEEN PRINTED THREE TIMES AND ALL THREE ARE HERE: this delivery's first printing said 1,210 and was wrong, the review measured 1,266 against the chapters as first written, and the delivered chapters now measure 1,156 because the repair took the repeated figure out of `290:91` and gave Chapters 283, 285 and 290 three fresh frames. 1,156 IS THE FIGURE THAT BELONGS TO THE FILES ON DISK.** **AND THE VOLUME'S TWO NON-PANEL TRACES WERE CITED AT `272:41` AND `289:57` AND NEITHER CITATION RESOLVED: `chapter-0272.md` HAS NO ITALIC LINE AT ALL AND THE BATCH 0003 TRACE IS AT `272:11`, WHICH IS THE SENTENCE ABOUT THE ROOM HE HAD STOPPED GOING TO, AND THE BATCH 0004 TRACE IS AT `289:63`.** Both are now cited correctly here and in `state/batch-summary.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and `workspace/volume-06/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`. **The budget is unchanged and it matters: there are TWO non-panel traces in the volume and Batch 0005 may not add a third.**

**THE ONE FINDING THAT WAS HALF RIGHT AND IS NOW SETTLED IN WRITING INSTEAD OF ARGUED. The Batch 0004 card asked for the spelled board figure *in a different shape each time*, and the delivery used one orthography in all ten chapters and ten frames, and the writer and the reviewer read the same words two ways.** The rule is now written in `outline/volume-06.md` and in the Batch 0005 prompt: **the figure is spelled in words in all ten chapters and never in digits, it stands in TEN DISTINCT FRAMES, no frame is used twice, and no figure appears twice in the band. The orthography is NOT varied and is NOT to be varied, because a woman of forty-one who has kept one number in one hand for twenty-five years does not spell the same number three ways, and a jitter in the spelling reads as a fault in the prose and not as a figure.**

**AND WHAT DID NOT CHANGE, so that a later pass does not go looking for a change: the batch's goal, its midpoint, its climax, the four accounts that do not agree, the sentence that is not said, the woman of about sixty who pays for it, the eleven families told in a yard by a woman who cannot name a day, the man of thirty-four's sentence, the register, the bell, the corridor's absence, the volume's question, the ending, and every character's age, sex, name and function.** The board chain still reads 63,714 / 63,721 / 63,728 / 63,741 / 63,745 / 63,751 / 63,759 / 63,763 / 63,770 / 63,774, spelled in words, in that order, and the ten files still run 11,416 to 15,708 bytes.

### AND THE RECEIPT OF THE REVIEW OF THIS DELIVERY, WHICH WAS RUN AGAINST THE CHAPTER FILES AND NOT AGAINST THE PROMPT

**A REVIEW WAS RUN AND IT RETURNED TWENTY-TWO FINDINGS. EIGHTEEN WERE REPAIRED IN PLACE AND FOUR ARE CARRIED WITH THE REASON. NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO CARD WAS MOVED, NO FIGURE ON THE PLANK WAS CHANGED, AND NO ARCHIVE BLOCK IN ANY OF THE SIX STATE FILES WAS EDITED. THE FULL LIST, WITH EVERY FINDING AND WHAT WAS DONE ABOUT IT, IS AT §10 OF THE TOP BLOCK OF `state/current.md` AND IS THE PLACE A REVIEWER SHOULD START.**

**AND THE FOUR THAT MATTERED MOST ARE WORTH NAMING HERE, BECAUSE THREE OF THEM ARE THE CLASSES THIS PROJECT HAS BEEN BITEN BY AND THE FOURTH IS A CLASS IT HAS NOT.**

1. **THE BATCH'S OWN FINDING WAS IN THE PROSE ONLY IN ITS NEGATED FORM**, while this file and the live record both had it as delivered. **A PARAGRAPH WAS ADDED AFTER THE WOMAN OF SEVENTY-FOUR'S WORDS, IN THE NARRATOR'S VOICE, which states the finding and then says plainly that she contradicts it and is right to.** `310:97`
2. **"FOUR HUNDRED MILES" HAD BEEN USED FOR THE MARKET TOWN, FOR THE PLANK AT COLDHARROW AND FOR A YARD, AND FOUR HUNDRED MILES IS THIS BOOK'S STANDING DISTANCE FOR DRAYMOOR AND FOR NOTHING ELSE.** Four places repaired. **A FIGURE THAT IS ALSO A CANON MARKER IS THE WORST KIND OF NUMBER IN THIS PROJECT, AND NO INSTRUMENT IN THIS REPOSITORY DISTINGUISHES THE TWO.**
3. **A MORNING'S FIGURE WAS BEING CARRIED BY A COURIER WHO COULD NOT HAVE HAD IT.** The rule in the prompt is that a figure may be stated away from the wall only by somebody who stood at that wall that morning, and the first writing broke that in Chapter 304 by three days. **THE FIGURE NOW COMES FROM A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT WHO WAS AT THE WALL AT THE SECOND LIGHT ON THAT MORNING, in Chapters 304, 307 and 308, and the courier, the bill and the day lost are gone.** **AN INSTRUMENT THAT CHECKS A FIGURE'S ARITHMETIC CANNOT CHECK WHOSE MOUTH IT CAME OUT OF, AND THE ONLY THING THAT CHECKS IT IS A READER WHO ASKS WHERE THE MAN WAS.**
4. **FIFTEEN PLACES WHERE THE NARRATOR WAS SPEAKING AS A DOCUMENT AND THE DECLARED META SWEEP COULD NOT SEE ANY OF THEM** — *in this book*, *in this band*, *in a volume*, *the chapter*, *in ten days*, *the whole of the next forty mornings*, *it is worth a paragraph*, and *there is no stage in this country for it*. **THE SWEEP IS NOW ZERO AND THE FOURTEEN FORMS ARE NAMED, AND THE LESSON IS THE THIRTEENTH TIME THIS PROJECT HAS LEARNED IT: A PATTERN THAT CANNOT SEE THE FORMS THE WRITER ACTUALLY USES RETURNS A CLEAN BAND ON A BAND WITH THE NOVELTY LEAKING OUT OF IT.** A later pass should run `in this book|in this band|in a volume|the chapter|in ten days|next ten days|next forty mornings|worth a paragraph|this project|this chapter|this volume|it takes to cross a yard|in a book in` over every chapter of every volume, and not the eleven words this project has been running since Volume 05.

**AND THE FOUR CARRIED, BRIEFLY: the four day-compositions that do not match the own-rings line, which is the inherited defect in the prompt's cards and cannot be fixed in prose; three of the six *about nine words*, because hunting all nineteen would be the same class of mistake as the script that destroyed seven files; the eight surviving *that is the whole of*, which are doing work at the ends of paragraphs; and the reviewer's judgement that Chapter 304 is the loosest chapter and the two riders in Chapter 306 are the most instrumental material in the band, which is carried because the riders ARE the price and a price that arrives by horse in two days is not instrumental, it is the town talking, and that is the only transport this country has.**
